denote the subgroup of elements which lie in
B_{G}
modulo
p^{\beta}
;

(2)

K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\subset H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the subgroup of elements
(h_{1},h_{2})\in H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which satisfy:

(h_{1},h_{2})\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&y\\
0&z\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&-y\\
0&z\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\quad\text{ modulo }p^{\beta}

for some
x,z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(which satisfies
K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})=\hat{\gamma}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
);

(3)

K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the subgroup of elements which lie in
B_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
modulo
p^{\beta}
.

Note that if
\operatorname{pr}_{i}\colon H\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}
is projection to one of the
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
-factors, then
\operatorname{pr}_{i}(K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}))=K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. We set

K^{G}_{\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\quad K^{H}_{\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(K^{p}\cap H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\quad K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}=\operatorname{pr}_{i}(K^{p}\cap H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})

for
i=1,2
(where
H=H_{1}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}H_{2}
).

3.1.2.

Let
\mathscr{G}\in\{G,H,\operatorname{GL}_{2}\}
. Then
Y_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
represents a certain moduli problem of principally polarised abelian varieties with extra structure. More precisely,
Y_{G}(K)
(resp.
Y_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K)
) parameterises principally polarised abelian surfaces (resp. elliptic curves) with
K
-level structure, and
Y_{H}(K)
parameterises pairs of elliptic curves with
K
-level structure. Over
Y_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
, we have a natural
\overline{P}_{\mathscr{G}}
-torsor

P_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR}}\to Y_{\mathscr{G}}(K)

which parameterises trivialisations of the first relative de Rham homology of the universal object over
Y_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
respecting the Hodge filtration. These torsors carry a
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant structure as the level
K
varies. These torsors (and their equivariant structure) extend to the toroidal compactifications
X_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
by considering trivialisations of the canonical extension of the first relative de Rham homology of the universal object – we will continue to denote this torsor by
P_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
.

Notation 3.1.2
.

For any (not necessarily finite-dimensional) algebraic representation
V
of
\overline{P}_{\mathscr{G}}
, we let

[V]=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes V\right)^{\overline{P}_{\mathscr{G}}}

denote the corresponding quasi-coherent sheaf on
X_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
. Here
\pi\colon P_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
denotes the structural map. If
V
is additionally a
(\operatorname{Lie}\mathscr{G},\overline{P}_{\mathscr{G}})
-module, then
[V]
comes equipped with an integrable connection with logarithmic poles along the boundary divisor
D_{\mathscr{G}}
.

3.1.3.

Since
K^{H}_{\beta}=\hat{\gamma}K^{G}_{\beta}\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we obtain a finite unramified morphism

\hat{\iota}\colon X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})\to X_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})

induced from right-translation by
\hat{\gamma}
(and the morphism of Shimura data between
H
and
G
). For any
\beta\geq 1
, the morphism
\hat{\iota}
is the pullback of the morphism for
\beta=1
with respect to the various forgetful maps; more precisely, one has a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}

for any
\beta\geq 1
(since both vertical maps have degree
p^{4}=[K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}):K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta+1})]=[K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}):K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta+1})]
). One can easily check that one has a reduction of structure
\hat{\iota}^{-1}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}=P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\times^{[\overline{P}_{H},\gamma]}\overline{P}_{G}
where the superscript denotes the pushout along the map
\overline{P}_{H}\hookrightarrow\overline{P}_{G}
,
h\mapsto\gamma^{-1}h\gamma
induced from conjugation by
\gamma^{-1}
(recall that
\gamma\in M_{G}
).

Similarly, since
K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}=\operatorname{pr}_{i}(K^{H}_{\beta})
, we obtain proper morphisms

\operatorname{pr}_{i}\colon X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})\twoheadrightarrow X_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta})

for
i=1,2
. One has a reduction of structure
\operatorname{pr}_{i}^{-1}P_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}=P_{\operatorname{dR},H}\times^{\overline{P}_{H}}\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
where the pushout is along the projection to the
i
-th factor.

3.2.
Coherent cohomology

We now introduce some coherent cohomology complexes.

Definition 3.2.1
.

Suppose that we are in Situation
2.2.2
. Let
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
and
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
denote the linear duals of
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
and
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
respectively. We set:

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\Gamma(X_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G}))

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\Gamma(X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{H}))

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\Gamma(X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})])

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}]).

We also use the notation
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})
,
R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})
,
R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})
,
R\Gamma(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})
for the cohomology complexes defined in exactly the same way but replacing
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
,
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
,
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
,
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
with
V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{*}
,
V_{M_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{*}
,
V_{M_{H}}(\kappa_{H})
,
V_{M_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})
respectively.

The cohomology groups of the complexes in Definition
3.2.1
should be viewed as certain spaces of “nearly holomorphic” automorphic forms for the corresponding reductive groups with fixed weight and bounded degree/order (with a cuspidality condition in the first two cases). For example, set

\mathscr{N}_{H_{i},\beta}^{\operatorname{nhol}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}),\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}}\right)

which one should view of as the space of all nearly holomorphic modular forms of level
K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}
without a fixed weight. This is naturally a
(\mathfrak{gl}_{2},\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}})
-module with an ascending filtration

\operatorname{Fil}_{i}\mathscr{N}_{H_{i},\beta}^{\operatorname{nhol}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{F\in\mathscr{N}_{H_{i},\beta}^{\operatorname{nhol}}:\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{i+1}\cdot F=0\right\},\quad\quad i\geq 0

with
\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathscr{N}_{H_{i},\beta}^{\operatorname{nhol}}
equal to the space of holomorphic modular forms of level
K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}
. The action of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is an algebraic interpretation of the Maass–Shimura differential operator and satisfies Griffiths transversality with respect to the above filtration. We then have identifications:

\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}=\operatorname{Hom}_{T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}\left(-\zeta_{H_{i}},\operatorname{Fil}_{t_{i}}\mathscr{N}^{\operatorname{nhol}}_{H_{i},\beta}\right),\quad\quad\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})=\operatorname{Hom}_{T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}\left(-\zeta_{H_{i}},\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathscr{N}^{\operatorname{nhol}}_{H_{i},\beta}\right).

As explained in
[
GPR23
, Remark 6.3.2]
,
\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
(resp.
\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})
) is identified with the usual space of nearly holomorphic forms of weight
1+t_{i}
, level
K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}
and degree
\leq t_{i}
(resp. degree
0
) after restricting weights to
\operatorname{SL}_{2}
and identifying the universal elliptic curve over
Y_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
with its dual via the principal polarisation.

3.2.1.

Suppose we are in Situation
2.2.2
. Then the reduction of structure for the groups
G
,
H
in §
3.1.3
and the map
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
induced from the dual of
\operatorname{br}^{\operatorname{nearly}}
induces a pullback map

\hat{\iota}^{*}\colon R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{\operatorname{nearly}}.

Similarly, the reduction of structure for the groups
H
,
H_{i}=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
in §
3.1.3
and the decomposition in Remark
2.2.4
induces an exterior cup product map

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{1}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{1}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{2}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{2}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}(\omega_{1})\smile\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{2}).

Finally, note that for any
i=0,1,2
, we have Serre duality pairings

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}\times\operatorname{H}^{2-i}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathbb{Q}.

induced from the natural duality pairing
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathbb{Q}
and the identification
[V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})]\cong\Omega^{2}_{X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})}(\operatorname{log}D_{H})
. We define the algebraic trilinear period as follows:

Definition 3.2.2
.

Suppose we are in Situation
2.2.2
and let
L
be a characteristic zero field. Then we let
\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{L}
denote the
L
-linear morphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{L}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})_{L}^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{1}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{1}})_{L}^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{2}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{2}})_{L}^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\to L

\displaystyle(\eta,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle\hat{\iota}^{*}\eta,\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}\rangle.

3.3.
The archimedean period

In this subsection, we describe the relation between
\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{\mathbb{C}}
and certain automorphic periods for automorphic forms of
G
and
H
. We first describe the relation between the cohomology complexes in Definition
3.2.1
and automorphic forms for the corresponding group. In this subsection, gothic letters will denote the corresponding complexified Lie algebras.

Let
\mathscr{A}(G)
denote the space of automorphic forms for
G
and let
\mathscr{A}_{0}(G)\subset\mathscr{A}(G)
denote the subspace of cuspforms. We use similar notation for
H
and
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. Let
K_{G,\infty}=\mathbb{R}^{\times}\cdot U_{2}(\mathbb{R})\subset G(\mathbb{R})
(resp.
K_{H,\infty}=Z_{H}(\mathbb{R})\cdot(U_{1}(\mathbb{R})\times U_{1}(\mathbb{R}))\subset H(\mathbb{R})
, resp.
K_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty}=\mathbb{R}^{\times}\cdot U_{1}(\mathbb{R})\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
) denote the standard maximal compact-mod-centre subgroups. For
\mathscr{G}\in\{G,H,\operatorname{GL}_{2}\}
, we set
K_{\mathscr{G},\infty}^{+}=K_{\mathscr{G},\infty}\cap\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
, where
\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{R})_{+}\subset\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the connected component of the identity in the
\mathbb{R}
-analytic topology. Let
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathscr{G}
denote the standard choices of Hodge cocharacters given by

\mu_{G}(z)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}z&&&\\
&z&&\\
&&1&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad\mu_{H}(z)=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}z&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}z&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right),\quad\mu_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(z)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}z&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right).

We let
K_{\mathscr{G},\infty}^{+}
act on any algebraic representation of
\overline{P}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{C})
through the map
K_{\mathscr{G},\infty}^{+}\hookrightarrow\overline{P}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{C})
given by conjugation by an element in
\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{C})
which transforms the Hodge cocharacter associated with
K_{\mathscr{G},\infty}^{+}
into the standard Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{\mathscr{G}}
.

Proposition 3.3.1

(Harris, Su)
.

There are identifications

(3.3.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{2}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{H}^{2}_{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G},K_{G,\infty}^{+})}\left(\mathscr{A}_{0}(G)^{K^{G}_{\beta}}\otimes\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\right)

(3.3.3)

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{H}^{0}_{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}},K_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty}^{+})}\left(\mathscr{A}(\operatorname{GL}_{2})^{K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\right)

where the right-hand side denotes the relative Lie algebra cohomology.

Proof.

This is the main result of
[
Su19
]
.
∎

The Lie algebra cohomology group in (
3.3.2
) can be calculated via the Chevalley–Eilenberg complex whose
j
-th term is

\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{G,\infty}^{+}}\left(\wedge^{j}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{\operatorname{nearly}},\mathscr{A}_{0}(G)^{K^{G}_{\beta}}\right).

We have a similar description for the Lie algebra cohomology group in (
3.3.3
). In particular, if
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{2}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{nearly}}
, then we have an associated
K_{G,\infty}^{+}
-equivariant morphism

\phi_{\eta}\colon\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathscr{A}_{0}(G)^{K^{G}_{\beta}}.

Similarly, if
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{nearly}}
, then we have an associated
K_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty}^{+}
-equivariant morphism:

F_{\omega_{i}}\colon\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{i}}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathscr{A}(\operatorname{GL}_{2})^{K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}}.

Let
[H]=\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})
, where
\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}\hookrightarrow Z_{H}(\mathbb{R})
is embedded diagonally, and let
dh
denote the Tamagawa measure on
[H]
. Let
\alpha_{i}\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H_{i}}
denote a choice of basis element defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, and set
\alpha_{1,2}=\alpha_{1}\otimes\alpha_{2}\in\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}=\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H_{1}}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H_{2}}
. The natural map
\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\hookrightarrow\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
sends
\alpha_{1,2}
to
\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2}
.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

With notation as above and as in §
2.2.2
:

•

set
\phi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\hat{\gamma}\cdot\phi_{\eta}((\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2})\otimes\jmath_{M_{G}}(w_{\kappa_{H}}))
, where
\hat{\gamma}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts through the natural map
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\hookrightarrow G(\mathbb{A})
;

•

for
i=1,2
, let
F_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}F_{\omega_{i}}(\upsilon_{\zeta_{H_{i}}}^{*})
.

Suppose that the morphism
\phi_{\eta}
factors through the morphism
\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{spl}_{M_{G}}\colon\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G})
. Then, up to a non-zero rational constant, we have

(3.3.5)

\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\eta,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=(2\pi i)^{-2}\operatorname{Vol}(\mathcal{K}_{\beta};dh)^{-1}\int_{[H]}\phi(h)F_{1}(h_{1})F_{2}(h_{2})dh

where
\mathcal{K}_{\beta}
denotes the image of
K^{+}_{H,\infty}K^{H}_{\beta}
in
[H]
.

Proof.

The pullback
\hat{\iota}^{*}(\eta)
is represented by the
K_{H,\infty}^{+}
-equivariant homomorphism:

\displaystyle\hat{\iota}^{*}\phi_{\eta}\colon\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{A}_{0}(H)^{K^{H}_{\beta}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{1,2}\otimes x

\displaystyle\mapsto\left.\left[\hat{\gamma}\cdot\phi_{\eta}((\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2})\otimes\gamma\cdot\operatorname{br}^{\operatorname{nearly}}(x))\right]\right|_{H(\mathbb{A})}.

Similarly, the exterior cup product
\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}
is represented by the
K_{H,\infty}^{+}
-equivariant homomorphism:

\displaystyle F_{\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}}\colon\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})^{*}=\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{1}}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\boxtimes\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{2}}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathscr{A}(H)^{K^{H}_{\beta}}

\displaystyle y\boxtimes z\mapsto\left((h_{1},h_{2})\mapsto F_{\omega_{1}}(y)(h_{1})F_{\omega_{2}}(z)(h_{2})\right).

Consider the
M_{H}
-equivariant morphism

\lambda\colon\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\xrightarrow{\gamma\cdot\operatorname{br}^{\operatorname{nearly}}}\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{spl}_{M_{G}}}V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G}).

This necessarily factors as

\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\xrightarrow{s^{\prime}}V_{M_{H}}(\kappa_{H})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{br}_{M}}V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G})

and
s^{\prime}
is the unique splitting of the natural map
V_{M_{H}}(\kappa_{H})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
. Fix a basis of
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
given by
\{e^{\prime}=\kappa_{H}\}\cup\{e_{j}:j\in J\}
, where
\kappa_{H}\in V_{M_{H}}(\kappa_{H})
is the canonical character, and
\{e_{j}\}
is a basis of the kernel of
s^{\prime}
. Let
\{(e^{\prime})^{*}\}\cup\{e_{j}^{*}:j\in J\}
denote the dual basis. Then the cup product
\hat{\iota}^{*}(\eta)\cup(\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2})
is represented by the
K_{H,\infty}^{+}
-equivariant morphism

\Xi\colon\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\otimes V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})^{*}\to\mathscr{A}_{0}(H)^{K^{H}_{\beta}}

given by

\Xi(\alpha_{1,2}\otimes x)=(\hat{\iota}^{*}\phi_{\eta})(\alpha_{1,2}\otimes e^{\prime})F_{\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}}((e^{\prime})^{*}\otimes x)+\sum_{j\in J}(\hat{\iota}^{*}\phi_{\eta})(\alpha_{1,2}\otimes e_{j})F_{\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}}(e_{j}^{*}\otimes x).

By the assumption that
\phi_{\eta}
factors through
\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{spl}_{M_{G}}
, we see that for
x
equal to the vector dual to the canonical basis of
V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})

\Xi(\alpha_{1,2}\otimes x)=(\hat{\iota}^{*}\phi_{\eta})(\alpha_{1,2}\otimes e^{\prime})F_{\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}}((e^{\prime})^{*}\otimes x)=\left((h_{1},h_{2})\mapsto\phi(h)F_{1}(h_{1})F_{2}(h_{2})\right)

where we have used the fact that
(e^{\prime})^{*}\otimes x=\upsilon_{\zeta_{H_{1}}}^{*}\boxtimes\upsilon_{\zeta_{H_{2}}}^{*}
. We can identify
(\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H})^{*}\otimes V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})
with the top exterior power of the sheaf of (real-analytic) differentials on
X_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})
, and the cup product
\hat{\iota}^{*}(\eta)\cup(\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2})
is therefore identified (up to non-zero rational scalar) with a
K_{H,\infty}^{+}
-invariant volume form

(h_{1},h_{2})\mapsto\phi(h)F_{1}(h_{1})F_{2}(h_{2})\cdot\alpha_{1,2}^{*}\otimes x^{*}.

The result now follows from
[
Har90
, Proposition 3.8]
.
∎

Remark 3.3.6
.

The non-zero rational constant in Proposition
3.3.4
is independent of the input data
(\eta,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
as well as the level subgroup
K^{H}_{\beta}
. Therefore, we can (and do) choose
\alpha_{i}
such that the equality (
3.3.5
) actually holds (i.e., not just up to rational scalar) for any choice of input data and level subgroup.

4.
Adic Shimura varieties

In this section, we construct a
p
-adic version of the trilinear pairing
\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}
by studying the coherent cohomology of the adic Shimura varieties associated with the groups
G
,
H
,
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. In this section, we work over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
but will often omit this from the notation.

4.1.
Geometry

For any
\mathscr{G}\in\{G,H,\operatorname{GL}_{2}\}
and sufficiently small compact open subgroup
K\subset\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we let
\mathcal{Y}_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{G}}(K)
) denote the adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
associated with
Y_{\mathscr{G}}(K)_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
(resp.
X_{\mathscr{G}}(K)_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
) via rigid analytification. We will define certain subspaces of these adic Shimura varieties via the Hodge–Tate period map.

4.1.1.

Suppose that
K=K^{p}K_{p}\subset\mathscr{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a sufficiently small compact open subgroup. As explained in
[
BP21
, §4.4.10]
, one has a (truncated) Hodge–Tate period map:

\pi_{\operatorname{HT},\mathscr{G},K_{p}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}\colon\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{G}}(K)\to\mathtt{FL}^{\mathscr{G}}/K_{p}

where
\mathtt{FL}^{\mathscr{G}}=P_{\mathscr{G}}^{\operatorname{an}}\backslash{\mathscr{G}}^{\operatorname{an}}
denotes the adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
associated with the (partial) flag variety
\operatorname{FL}_{\mathscr{G}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}P_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathscr{G}
(here the rational polyhedral cone decomposition is chosen to be independent of
K_{p}
so that
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathcal{X}_{\mathscr{G}}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})
exists as a perfectoid space). The morphism
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},\mathscr{G},K_{p}}
is functorial in the level
K_{p}
and Shimura datum.

4.1.2.

Let
{{}^{M}W_{\mathscr{G}}}
denote the set of minimal length representatives for
W_{M_{\mathscr{G}}}\backslash W_{\mathscr{G}}
. Then one has a Bruhat decomposition:

\operatorname{FL}_{\mathscr{G},\mathbb{F}_{p}}=\bigcup_{w\in{{}^{M}W_{\mathscr{G}}}}P_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\left(P_{\mathscr{G}}\cdot w\cdot B_{\mathscr{G}}\right)=\bigcup_{w\in{{}^{M}W_{\mathscr{G}}}}C_{w}^{\mathscr{G}}.

Let
X^{\mathscr{G}}_{w}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\cup_{w^{\prime}\leq w}C_{w^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{G}}
and
Y^{\mathscr{G}}_{w}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\cup_{w^{\prime}\geq w}C_{w^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{G}}
where
\leq
denotes the Bruhat ordering on
{{}^{M}W_{\mathscr{G}}}
. For any locally closed subset
Z\subset\operatorname{FL}_{\mathscr{G},\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, we let
{]Z[}\subset\mathtt{FL}^{\mathscr{G}}
denote the tube of
Z
in the adic flag variety, i.e.,
{]Z[}
is defined to be the interior of the preimage of
Z
under the specialisation map
\operatorname{sp}\colon\mathtt{FL}^{\mathscr{G}}\to\operatorname{FL}_{\mathscr{G},\mathbb{F}_{p}}
. For any
m,n\in\mathbb{Q}\cup\{-\infty\}
, we let
{]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{w}[_{m,n}}
,
]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{w}[_{\overline{m},n}
, etc. denote the (partially compactified) tubes of varying radii introduced in
[
BP21
, §3.3.6]
.

Definition 4.1.1
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer.

(1)

For
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, let
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}\subset\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})
denote the quasi-compact open subspace given by the interior of

\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\right).

We also let
\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}\subset\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})
denote the closed subset given by the closure of
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{K}K_{p}\right)
where
\mathcal{K}\subset G^{\operatorname{an}}
denotes the analytic upper-triangular Klingen parabolic subgroup, i.e., for
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the group
\mathcal{K}(S)
is equal to all elements in
G(R^{+})
of the form
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*&*&*\\
&*&*&*\\
&*&*&*\\
&&&*\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Note that
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}
.

(2)

For
K_{p}=K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
K_{p}=K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
), let
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}\subset\mathcal{X}_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}\subset\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta})
) denote the quasi-compact open subspace given by the interior of

\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(]C^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\right)\quad\quad\left(\text{ resp. }\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(]C^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{id}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\right)\right).

Let
\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}\subset\mathcal{X}_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})
(resp.
\mathcal{Z}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}\subset\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta})
) denote the closure of
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
).

Remark 4.1.2
.

The subspaces in Definition
4.1.1
have more tractable descriptions away from the boundary and over the good reduction locus. More precisely (over the good reduction locus and away from the boundary) the subspace
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
parameterises abelian surfaces
A
(with a polarisation and prime-to-
p
level structure) with good ordinary reduction and a filtration

0\subset H_{1}\subset H_{2}=H_{2}^{\perp}\subset H_{1}^{\perp}\subset A[p]

by cyclic finite flat subgroup schemes (with
\operatorname{rk}H_{i}=p^{i\beta}
) such that
H_{1}
(resp.
H_{2}/H_{1}
) extends to a multiplicative (resp. étale) group scheme over the integral model. The subspace
\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}
is then the closure of the locus parameterising abelian surfaces
A
(with polarisation and prime-to-
p
level structure) and
0\subset H_{1}\subset H_{2}
as above, such that
H_{1}
extends to a multiplicative group scheme.

Similarly,
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
is the canonical ordinary locus inside the modular curve, i.e., over the good reduction locus and away from the boundary it parameterises elliptic curves
E
with a cyclic rank
p^{\beta}
subgroup scheme
C_{i}\subset E[p^{\beta}]
such that
C_{i}
extends to a multiplicative group scheme over the integral model of
E
. The subspace
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
is the finite étale cover of
\mathcal{X}_{H_{1},\operatorname{id},\beta}\times\mathcal{X}_{H_{2},\operatorname{id},\beta}
parameterising isomorphisms
C_{1}\cong C_{2}
.

Via an explicit computation on the level of flag varieties (c.f., the proof of
[
LZ21a
, Proposition 5.2.1]
), one has
\hat{\iota}(\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta})\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
and
\hat{\iota}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta})=\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
. Similarly one clearly has
\operatorname{pr}_{i}(\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta})\subset\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
for any
i=1,2
.

4.2.
Torsors

The goal of this section is to obtain certain reductions of structure of
P_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR}}
which will allow one to construct Banach sheaves locally modelled on the representations in §
2.3
. To do this, we first have to introduce certain partially compactified Igusa varieties over the components of the ordinary locus introduced in the previous section.

4.2.1.

We now review the construction of the partially compactified Igusa varieties in
[
BP
, §3.4]
for the group
G
. We fix a good
K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-admissible cone decomposition underlying the toroidal compactifications we consider in this section. Note that we can choose the cone decomposition in such a way that it is good for any finite index subgroup of
K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
3
3

3

See
[
CS24
, §2.5]
for example.
Let
\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
denote the formal completion of the toroidal compactification
X_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(as constructed in
[
Lan13
]
) along the special fibre, and let
\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
denote the ordinary locus. Let
\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\subset\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
denote the formal completion of the open Shimura variety, and
\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}\subset\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
the ordinary locus.

Consider the “standard ordinary”
p
-divisible group
\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mu_{p^{\infty}}^{\oplus 2}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)^{\oplus 2}
over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, which we equip with the symplectic pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}\times\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}\to\mu_{p^{\infty}}
defined by the matrix
J
(see §
2.1
). We let
J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
denote the fpqc group scheme over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
which on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebras
R
for which
p
is nilpotent is described as:

J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}(R)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Aut}_{R}\left((\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},R},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle)\right)

i.e., automorphisms of
\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},R}
preserving the symplectic pairing
up to similitude
in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.
4
4

4

There is a minor clash of notation between the matrix
J
and the group
J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
, however we will not mention the former again in this section.
This can be described as the semi-direct product
J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}=U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})\rtimes M(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
, where
M(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
is the pro-étale group scheme given by
\underline{M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
and the unipotent part
U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
is isomorphic to the abelian group scheme of
2\times 2
matrices
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x_{11}&x_{12}\\
x_{21}&x_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with coefficients in
T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}
satisfying
x_{11}=x_{22}
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}
denote the fpqc
J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
-torsor parameterising trivialisations
\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord}}\xrightarrow{\sim}A[p^{\infty}]
respecting polarisations up to similitude, where
A
denotes the universal ordinary abelian surface over
\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}
.

For any finite index fpqc subgroup scheme
J_{p}\subset J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
, we set
U(J_{p})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}J_{p}\cap U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
and
M(J_{p})=J_{p}/U(J_{p})\hookrightarrow M(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
, and we let
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})
denote the categorical quotients of
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}
by
U(J_{p})
and
J_{p}
respectively. Note that the natural map

\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})

is a pro-étale
M(J_{p})
-torsor (see
[
BP
, Proposition 3.4.9(5)]
). We let
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))
and
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})
denote the adic generic fibres of
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})
respecively.

Note that
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}))\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})=\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}
is the usual Mantovan Igusa variety parameterising trivialisations of the connected and étale parts of the
p
-divisible group
A[p^{\infty}]
respecting the polarisation up to similitude. This moduli problem is equivalent to trivialising the connected part
A[p^{\infty}]^{\circ}
and making a choice of similitude factor in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
; since
A[p^{\infty}]^{\circ}
extends to a
p
-divisible group over
\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}
, we therefore see that
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}))
extends to a pro-étale
M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsor
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}
. The Mantovan Igusa variety carries a natural lift of Frobenius
\varphi\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}))\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}))
by quotienting by the canonical subgroup, and this extends to
\varphi\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}))^{\operatorname{tor}}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
.

We now define
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}^{\operatorname{tor}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varprojlim_{\varphi}\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
where the inverse limit has transition maps given by
\varphi
. This exists as a formal scheme because
\varphi
is affine, and one has

\mathfrak{IG}_{G}=\mathfrak{IG}_{G}^{\operatorname{tor}}\times_{\mathfrak{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}}\mathfrak{Y}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}.

By
[
BP
, Proposition 3.4.16]
, the action of
J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
extends to
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}^{\operatorname{tor}}
and for any finite index fpqc subgroup scheme
J_{p}\subset J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
, the categorical quotients
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}=\mathfrak{IG}_{G}^{\operatorname{tor}}/U(J_{p})
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}=\mathfrak{IG}_{G}^{\operatorname{tor}}/J_{p}
exist. Moreover, the map
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is a pro-étale
M(J_{p})
-torsor. As above, we let
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
and
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
denote the adic generic fibres of
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
respectively.

4.2.2.

Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
denote the
p
-adic completion of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})
, and let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
denote its ring of integers. Let
J_{\operatorname{ord},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and let
J_{\operatorname{ord},\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}^{+}=J_{\operatorname{ord},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{+}\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
. We use similar notation for the other groups considered in the previous subsection. Fix an identification
T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
. Depending on this identification, we have an isomorphism
J^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}\cong P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Let
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be a compact open subgroup and let
J_{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w_{1}K_{p}w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(which is a compact open subgroup of
P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
). By abuse of notation, we also let
J_{p}
denote the schematic closure of
J_{p}
inside
J^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}=J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}\times\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
(which is a finite-index fpqc subgroup scheme – see
[
BP
, Lemma 3.4.8]
). One has a natural open immersion

(4.2.2)

\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}

induced from right-translation by
w_{1}
, and this extends to an open immersion
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}
. Here
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})
denotes the adic space associated with
X_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and
\mathcal{Y}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})\subset\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})
denotes the good reduction locus away from the boundary.

If
J_{p}
splits as a semidirect product
J_{p}=U(J_{p})\rtimes M(J_{p})
, then
J_{p}
is Galois stable and descends to a compact open subgroup of
J_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
(see
[
BP
, Remark 3.4.7]
). In this setting, the quotient
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})
is therefore defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, however it may still be the case that (
4.2.2
) is only defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{m}})
for some integer
m\geq 0
. If we assume that
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset K_{p}
though, then both (
4.2.2
) and its extension to the (partial) toroidal compactifications descend to open immersions over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

In practice, we will only consider
K_{p}
such that
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset K_{p}
and
J_{p}=w_{1}K_{p}w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
splits as a semidirect product. For example, for any
\beta\geq 1
and
t\in T^{G,\pm}
, the compact open subgroup
K_{p}=tK^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})t^{-1}\cap K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
satisfies both of these properties.

Example 4.2.3
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer. Then
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is identified with
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
.

4.2.3.

We now suppose that
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is a compact open subgroup such that
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset K_{p}
and
J_{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}w_{1}K_{p}w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
splits as a semidirect product (as in the previous section). Recall that
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}\to\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is a pro-étale
M(J_{p})
-torsor. Over
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))
one has a universal trivialisation
\mu_{p^{\infty}}^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{\sim}A[p^{\infty}]^{\circ}
of the connected part of the
p
-divisible group associated with the universal ordinary abelian surface. This induces a trivialisation of
\omega_{A}
which extends to a trivialisation of the canonical extension of
\omega_{A}
over
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
. Combining this with the choice of symplectic similitude, we therefore obtain a trivialisation of the graded pieces of the Hodge filtration on
\mathcal{H}
, where
\mathcal{H}
denotes the pullback to
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
of the canonical extension of
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(A/\mathcal{Y}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}))
. Since the unit root splitting extends to the canonical extension
\mathcal{H}
, we therefore obtain a trivialisation of
\mathcal{H}
over
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
respecting the Hodge filtration and polarisation up to similitude. In other words, we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})}

where the bottom horizontal arrow denotes the extension of (
4.2.2
) to partial toroidal compactifications. The top horizontal map is equivariant for the action of
M(J_{p})
(via the embedding
M(J_{p})\hookrightarrow M_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\hookrightarrow\overline{P}_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}
) and hence defines a reduction of structure over
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
.

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer. Then there exists a quasi-compact open strict neighbourhood
U
of
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
inside
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})
and an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n}M(J_{p})
-torsor
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}\to U
such that:

•

\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}
is a reduction of structure of
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
over
U
;

•

\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
is a reduction of structure of
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}
over
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
.

Moreover, if we view this torsor as an open subspace of
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
, then this torsor (with these properties) is unique up to possibly shrinking
U
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is very similar to that of
[
GPR23
, Proposition 6.2.1]
. We first claim that there exists a sheaf
\mathcal{L}
of
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})}
-modules, which is locally free of rank one, and a section
h\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}),\mathcal{L}/p\mathcal{L})
such that
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is the locus of points
|\cdot|_{x}
satisfying
|\widetilde{h}|_{x}=1
for any local lift
\widetilde{h}
of
h
. Indeed,
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is a connected component of
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})^{\operatorname{ord}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})\times_{\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))}\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))^{\operatorname{ord}}
, hence there exists a section
h^{\prime}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})^{\operatorname{ord}},\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})^{\operatorname{ord}}})
such that
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is the locus in
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})^{\operatorname{ord}}
where
|h^{\prime}|=1
. Let
\operatorname{Ha}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}),\operatorname{det}(\omega_{A}^{+})^{\otimes p-1})
be a lift of the Hasse invariant. There then exists a sufficiently large integer
N\geq 1
and section
h^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}),\operatorname{det}(\omega_{A}^{+})^{\otimes N(p-1)}/p)
such that
h^{\prime\prime}
is congruent to
\operatorname{Ha}^{N}h^{\prime}
modulo
p
over
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})^{\operatorname{ord}}
. We may then take
\mathcal{L}=\operatorname{det}(\omega_{A}^{+})^{\otimes(N+1)(p-1)}
and
h=\operatorname{Ha}h^{\prime\prime}
. The upshot of this is that we have a cofinal system
\{\mathcal{X}_{r}\}_{r\geq 1}
of strict quasi-compact open neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
in
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})
with
\mathcal{X}_{r}
equal to the locus of points
|\cdot|_{x}
satisfying
|\widetilde{h}|_{x}^{p^{r+1}}\geq|p|
.

Let
\mathscr{G}_{k}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,k}M(J_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,k}
which is also equal to the image of
M(J_{p})
in
M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
. Let
\mathcal{I}_{k,\infty}
denote the quotient of
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
by the kernel of
M(J_{p})\to\mathscr{G}_{k}
. Then
\mathcal{I}_{k,\infty}\to\mathcal{X}_{\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is a finite étale
\mathscr{G}_{k}
-torsor. We also let
\mathfrak{I}_{k,\infty}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
denote the finite étale
\mathscr{G}_{k}
-torsor given by the quotient of
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}
by the kernel of
M(J_{p})\to\mathscr{G}_{k}
. Let
C_{k}\subset A[p^{k}]
denote the canonical subgroup of order
p^{2k}
. This extends to the boundary and there exists an integer
r
such that
C_{k}
extends to
\mathcal{X}_{r}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{k,r}\to\mathcal{X}_{r}
denote the finite étale
M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
-torsor parameterising trivialisations of
C_{r}
and a choice of similitude. We have a natural
\mathscr{G}_{k}
-equivariant map
\mathcal{I}_{k,\infty}\to\mathcal{F}_{k,r}
which provides a reduction of structure over
\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
, hence by the arguments in
[
KL05
, Proposition 2.2.1]
, we see that there exists a sufficiently large integer
r
and a finite étale
\mathscr{G}_{k}
-torsor
\mathcal{I}_{k,r}\to\mathcal{X}_{r}
fitting into the following Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}_{k,\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}_{k,r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{r}}

where the top map is
\mathscr{G}_{k}
-equivariant.

The rest of the proof now follows
[
GPR23
, Proposition 6.2.1]

mutatis mutandis
by first defining a
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n}
-torsor over
\mathcal{I}_{k,\infty}
and showing that this descends to an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n}M(J_{p})
-torsor over
\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
, then using the fact that
\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
is the locus where
|\widetilde{h}|=1
and the above Cartesian diagram to overconverge this torsor.
∎

Remark 4.2.5
.

We note that there is some ambiguity when referring to “the” reduction of structure
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}
, since this torsor depends on
U
(and may not be unique over
U
). It is however unique up to shrinking
U
, and since the majority of the objects considered later on are up to shrinking
U
, we can safely pretend that
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}
is unique.

4.2.4.

Exactly the same constructions in the previous sections carry over to the setting of Shimura varieties for
H
or
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
(replacing the element
w_{1}
with the identity element). In particular, we will use similar notation for the corresponding objects for the groups
H
,
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. We note that, for
\beta\geq 1
, it is not true that
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
– instead one only has
T_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\subset K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, however this still implies that
\mathcal{IG}_{H}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{H}(K^{p}K_{p})
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
for any
K_{p}\subset H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of the form
tK^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})t^{-1}\cap K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
with
t\in T^{H,\pm}
. In particular, we have an analogue of Proposition
4.2.4
– we denote the corresponding reductions of structure by
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},n}
for
\mathscr{G}\in\{H,\operatorname{GL}_{2}\}
. Note that when
\mathscr{G}=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
and
K_{p}=H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, this is nothing but
[
GPR23
, Proposition 6.2.1]
.

4.2.5.
Maps between torsors

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer. For ease of notation set
\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{1},\beta}^{\operatorname{tor}}=\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
(resp.
\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}^{\operatorname{tor}}=\mathcal{IG}_{H}((K^{p}\cap H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
) for
J_{p}=w_{1}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
J_{p}=K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\cap P_{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
). Then we have a commutative cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}^{\operatorname{tor}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{1},\beta}^{\operatorname{tor}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}

where the horizontal maps in the top square are induced from right-translation by
\gamma
(recall that
\gamma\in M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
). The bottom square is Cartesian, and since
\hat{\iota}
is a finite morphism between qcqs adic spaces, one can show that
\{\hat{\iota}^{-1}(U)\}
is a cofinal collection of quasi-compact open subspaces containing the closure of
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
as
U
runs over quasi-compact open subspaces of
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})
containing the closure of
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
.
5
5

5

Indeed, it suffices to check
\bigcap_{U}\hat{\iota}^{-1}(U)
and
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
have the same rank one points, but this follows from the fact that
\left(\cap_{U}U\right)^{\operatorname{rk}1}=\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}^{\operatorname{rk}1}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}\to U
be the reduction of structure of
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
as constructed in Proposition
4.2.4
. Then we can consider the pullback
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}\times_{P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR}},\gamma}P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to\hat{\iota}^{-1}U
, which is an étale torsor under the group
\gamma\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}\gamma^{-1}\cap\overline{P}_{H}^{\operatorname{an}}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}
. Therefore, by the uniqueness part of (the analogue of) Proposition
4.2.4
for
H
, we can find a sufficiently small
U
and a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{H,\operatorname{dR},n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{-1}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}

which provides a reduction of structure
\mathcal{P}_{H,\operatorname{dR},n}\times^{[\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit},\gamma]}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}\cong\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}\times_{U}\hat{\iota}^{-1}(U)
.

We also have morphisms between the reductions of structure of
P_{\bullet,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
for
\bullet\in\{H,\operatorname{GL}_{2}\}
. More precisely, one can show that for any quasi-compact open subspace
U\subset\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta})
containing the closure of
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
such that one has a reduction of structure
\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},n}\to U
, there exists a sufficiently small quasi-compact open subspace
V\subset\mathcal{X}_{H}(K^{H}_{\beta})
containing the closure of
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
and a reduction of structure
\mathcal{P}_{H,\operatorname{dR},n}\to V
fitting into a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{H,\operatorname{dR},n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{i}}

Here the top arrow is compatible with the natural map
P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to P_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
induced from the projection
\operatorname{pr}_{i}\colon H\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{2}
and provides a reduction of structure.

4.3.
p-adic nearly sheaves

We now consider the sheaves associated with the torsors in Proposition
4.2.4
.

Notation 4.3.1
.

Let
(A,A^{+})
be a complete Tate affinoid pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. For any projective Banach
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
-representation
V
over
(A,A^{+})
, we let

[V]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}}\hat{\otimes}V\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}}

denote the associated sheaf on
U\times\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
, where
\pi\colon\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}\to U
denotes the reduction of structure in Proposition
4.2.4
. We use similar notation for
H
,
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
and projective Banach representations of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}
,
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n,\beta}^{\square}
. Note that
[V]
is a locally projective Banach sheaf by a similar argument as in
[
BP21
, Proposition 6.3.3]
.

Remark 4.3.2
.

The sheaves
[V]
are only independent of choices up to shrinking
U
because the same is true for the torsors
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}
. Since we will only interested in the cohomology of these sheaves modulo shrinking
U
, this ambiguity will not affect the constructions in the following sections.

If
V
(resp.
W
) is a projective Banach
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
-representation (resp.
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}
-representation) and we have a continuous morphism
f\colon V\to W
which is equivariant for the action of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}
through the inclusion
\gamma^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}\gamma\subset\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
, then the discussion in §
4.2.5
implies that we have a natural morphism
[V]\to\hat{\iota}_{*}[W]
for
U
sufficiently small induced from
f
(and
[W]
is defined over
\hat{\iota}^{-1}U
). Similarly, if
W_{i}
is a projective Banach
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n,\beta}^{\square}
-representation and
g\colon W_{i}\to W
is equivariant through the map
\operatorname{pr}_{i}\colon\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}\to\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n,\beta}^{\square}
, then we obtain a morphism
[W_{i}]\to(\operatorname{pr}_{i})_{*}[W]
induced from
g
, where
\operatorname{pr}_{i}\colon V\to U
is as in §
4.2.5
.

We will consider the above sheaves for a small modification of the specific representations appearing in §
2.3
. More precisely:

Notation 4.3.3
.

Let
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square,\circ}}(\kappa_{G})
be defined in exactly the same way as in Definition
2.3.3
, but replacing
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
with the subgroup
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square,\circ}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\circ}_{G,n}M_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\subset\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
where
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\circ}_{G,n}\subset\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n}
denotes the subgroup of elements which are congruent to the identity modulo
p^{n+\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon>0
. We let
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G})
denote the continuous dual of
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square,\circ}}(\kappa_{G})
, which we view as a projective Banach
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}
-representation.

Similarly, we let
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n+1,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})
denote the continuous dual of
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit,\circ}}(\kappa_{H})
, which is a projective Banach
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n+1,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}
-module, where
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit,\circ}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n}^{\circ}T_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. We will also use similar notation for the versions
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G})
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{H,n+1,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})
defined using the Levi subgroups.

Remark 4.3.4
.

If the action of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
factors through
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}\to\mathcal{M}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
, then
[V]
agrees with the sheaf constructed in
[
BP21
, §6.3]
after possibly shrinking
U
. In the setting of
loc.cit.
, one can be more precise about the locus over which
[V]
is defined (however we will not need this).

4.3.1.
Cohomological correspondences

Fix an integer
\beta\geq 1
and let
t\in T^{G,-}
. Set
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
and
J_{p}=w_{1}K_{p}w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and set
K_{p}^{\prime}=tK_{p}t^{-1}\cap K_{p}
,
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}=t^{-1}K_{p}^{\prime}t
,
J_{p}^{\prime}=(w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1})J_{p}(w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1})^{-1}\cap J_{p}=w_{1}K_{p}^{\prime}w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and
J_{p}^{\prime\prime}=(w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1})^{-1}J_{p}^{\prime}(w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1})=w_{1}K_{p}^{\prime\prime}w_{1}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where
p_{1}
,
q_{1}
are the natural forgetful maps,
p_{2}
is induced from right-translation by
t
,
q_{2}
is induced from right-translation by
w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1}
, and the vertical arrows are induced from right-translation by
w_{1}
. Note that
p_{1}
,
p_{2}
,
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
are all finite flat. The squares in this diagram are often not Cartesian, however we have the following property:

Lemma 4.3.5
.

With notation as above, one has

p_{1}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}\right)\cap p_{2}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}\right)=\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{tor}},

where the intersection takes place in
\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Clearly
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is contained in this intersection, so it suffices to prove the reverse inclusion. Furthermore, it suffices to check this on rank one points. Consider the truncated Hodge–Tate period map

\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K^{\prime}_{p})\to\mathtt{FL}^{G}/K_{p}^{\prime}.

Then (on rank one points):

•

p_{1}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}\right)
is equal to the intersection over all
k\geq 1
of
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}\left({]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}}K_{p}\right)
;

•

p_{2}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}\right)
is equal to the intersection over all
k\geq 1
of
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}\left({]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}}K_{p}t^{-1}\right)
.

It therefore suffices to show that, for any
k\geq 1
, there exists
l\gg k
such that

{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{l,l}}K_{p}\cap{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{l,l}}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}}K_{p}^{\prime}.

Let
t=\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},t_{2},\nu t_{2}^{-1},\nu t_{1}^{-1})
and let
l\gg\beta
and
l\gg\operatorname{max}(t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-\operatorname{min}\{v_{p}(\alpha(t)):\alpha\in\Phi_{G}^{+}\}
. By the Iwahori factorisation for
K_{p}
, we see that an element
X\in{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{l,l}}K_{p}
is uniquely represented by

w_{1}\cdot\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
x_{1}&1&y&\\
&&1&\\
x_{2}&&-x_{1}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)

with
x_{1},x_{2}\in p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}+\mathcal{B}_{l}^{\circ}
,
y\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Similarly,
{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{l,l}}K_{p}t^{-1}
is uniquely represented by

w_{1}\cdot\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
t_{2}t_{1}^{-1}x^{\prime}_{1}&1&\nu^{-1}t_{2}^{2}y^{\prime}&\\
&&1&\\
\nu t_{1}^{-2}x^{\prime}_{2}&&-t_{2}t_{1}^{-1}x^{\prime}_{1}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right).

with
x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}\in p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}+\mathcal{B}_{l}^{\circ}
,
y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Both of these are unique representations of the elements in the translate of the big Bruhat cell by
w_{1}
, hence if
X\in{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{l,l}}K_{p}\cap{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{l,l}}K_{p}t^{-1}
, we must have
x_{1}=t_{2}t_{1}^{-1}x_{1}^{\prime}
,
x_{2}=\nu t_{1}^{-2}x_{2}^{\prime}
and
y=\nu^{-1}t_{2}^{2}y^{\prime}
, hence
X\in{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}}K_{p}^{\prime}
as required.
∎

Remark 4.3.6
.

At no point in the proof of Lemma
4.3.5
did we use the fact that
t\in T^{G,-}
, and the proof also applies when
t\in T^{G,+}
.

As a consequence of Lemma
4.3.5
, we see that
\{p_{1}^{-1}(U)\cap p_{2}^{-1}(U^{\prime})\}
forms a cofinal system of quasi-compact open neighbourhoods of the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{tor}}
as
U
,
U^{\prime}
run through quasi-compact open neighbourhoods of the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
. Let

. We can therefore find sufficiently small
U
,
U^{\prime}
such that we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n+d,K_{p}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{t^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n+d,K_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n,K_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{-1}(U)\cap p_{2}^{-1}(U^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{t}}

where the vertical arrows are reductions of structure (here we have temporarily included the level subgroup in the notation and
t\colon\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime})
denotes the map induced from right-translation by
t
). The top horizontal arrow is induced from the morphism
\phi_{t}\colon p_{1}^{-1}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{2}^{-1}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
given by the composition of the equivariant structure and right-translation by
w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1}
(through the torsor structure). This morphism fits into the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}^{\prime}))^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n+d,K_{p}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{1}^{-1}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{-1}\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}U(J_{p}^{\prime\prime}))^{\operatorname{tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{t^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{2}^{-1}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{t}}

Definition 4.3.7
.

Let
V
be projective Banach
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square}
-representation over
(A,A^{+})
which is additionally equipped with an action of
w_{1}T^{G,-}w_{1}^{-1}
. Then we let
(w_{1}^{-1}\star\mathbf{1})(t)\psi_{t}\colon p_{2}^{*}[V]\to p_{1}^{*}[V]
denote the morphism (defined over
p_{1}^{-1}(U)\cap p_{2}^{-1}(U^{\prime})
for sufficiently small
U
,
U^{\prime}
as above) given by

p_{2}^{*}[V]=\left((\pi^{\prime\prime})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{t^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}}\hat{\otimes}V\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n}M(J_{p}^{\prime\prime})}\xrightarrow{\phi_{t}^{*}\otimes(w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1})\cdot-}\left((\pi^{\prime})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n+d,K_{p}^{\prime}}}\hat{\otimes}V\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+d}M(J_{p}^{\prime})}=p_{1}^{*}[V]

where

denote the structural maps.

Remark 4.3.8
.

The optimal normalisation of the trace map associated with
p_{1}
in neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{IG}_{G}(K^{p}J_{p})^{\operatorname{tor}}
is
(w_{1}^{-1}\star\mathbf{1})(t)^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}
, which explains the appearance of the factor in the above definition.

4.4.
Cohomology

We now discuss various overconvergent cohomology groups.

4.4.1.
Classical weight overconvergent cohomology

We first introduce overconvergent cohomology complexes associated with the algebraic “nearly” representations appearing in Definition
3.2.1
. So the notation is not overloaded, we will henceforth use the notation “
\ddagger
” to mean “nearly overconvergent”.

Definition 4.4.1
.

Suppose that we are in Situation
2.2.2
. We set:

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,-)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}\cap U}(U,[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G}))

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,-)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}}(U,[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{H}))

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})^{(\ddagger,+)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma(U,[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\otimes V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})])

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{(\ddagger,+)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma(U,[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}]).

where the colimit is over all strict neighbourhoods
U
of
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
(in the first case),
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
(in the second and third cases),
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
(in the fourth case). We define the complexes
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,-)}
,
R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,-)}
,
R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})^{(\dagger,+)}
,
R\Gamma(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{(\dagger,+)}
in exactly the same way replacing the nearly representations with the corresponding algebraic representations of the Levi subgroup.

The complexes in Definition
4.4.1
carry an action of Hecke operators away from
p
as well as actions of
T^{G,-}
,
T^{H,-}
,
T^{H,+}
,
T^{H_{i},+}
respectively through Hecke operators at
p
(the
\pm
-superscript denotes the relevant submonoid). For example, for the first complex the Hecke action at

is constructed using the (unnormalised) cohomological correspondence
6
6

6

Or more simply the algebraic version over
X_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})
.

(w_{1}^{-1}\star\mathbf{1})(t)\psi_{t}
in Definition
4.3.7
in combination with the following lemma (using the general process described in
[
Gra24
, §4]
for example).

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Set
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Let
\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(\overline{]X^{G}_{\operatorname{id}}[}\right)
and
\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(\overline{]X^{G}_{w_{1}}[}\right)
. Consider the correspondence associated with
t\in T^{G,-}

(4.4.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where
K_{p}^{\prime}=tK_{p}t^{-1}\cap K_{p}
. Set
T(-)=p_{2}p_{1}^{-1}(-)
and
T^{t}(-)=p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(-)
. Then one has:

(1)

\bigcap_{m\geq 1}(T^{t})^{m}(\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{2})=\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}
;

(2)

\bigcap_{n,m\geq 1}T^{n}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})-\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-})\cap(T^{t})^{m}(\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{2})=\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
;

(3)

both
(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p}),\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-})
and
(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{p}K_{p})-\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-},\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-})
are open/closed support conditions for the correspondence (
4.4.3
), in the sense of
[
BP
, §6.1.1]
.

Proof.

This follows from the same strategy as in
[
BP
, Proposition 6.1.11]
(although note that
loc.cit.
is for the “
+
”-version of higher Coleman theory). More precisely, parts (2) and (3) follow immediately from the analogous “
-
”-version of the proof in
loc.cit.
. For part (1), the proof in
loc.cit.
shows that
\bigcap_{m\geq 1}\overline{]X^{G}_{w_{1}}[}K_{p}t^{-m}K_{p}
is equal to the closure of
\mathcal{X}_{w_{1}}^{G}K_{p}
, where
\mathcal{X}_{w_{1}}^{G}\subset\mathtt{FL}^{G}
denotes the analytic closed subset (the analytic version of
X_{w_{1}}^{G}\subset\mathrm{FL}_{G,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
). But one can easily observe that
\mathcal{X}^{G}_{w_{1}}=P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\backslash P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{K}
, where
\mathcal{K}
denotes the analytic Klingen parabolic as in Definition
4.1.1
.
∎

4.4.2.
p
-adic weight overconvergent cohomology

We now define the analogous cohomology complexes as in Definition
4.4.1
for the
p
-adic representations appearing in §
2.3
.

Definition 4.4.4
.

Let
(A,A^{+})
be a complete Tate affinoid pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
n\geq 1
be an integer and let
\kappa_{G},\kappa_{H}\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to A^{\times}
and
\zeta_{H_{i}}\colon T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to A^{\times}
be
n
-analytic characters. We set:

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}\cap U}(U,[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G})](-D_{G}))

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}}(U,[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n+1,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})](-D_{H}))

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma(U,[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{H,n,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}}(\kappa_{H})\otimes V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H})])

\displaystyle R\Gamma(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma(U,[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})]).

where the colimit is over all strict neighbourhoods
U
of
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}
(in the first case),
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
(in the second and third cases),
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
(in the fourth case) over which the sheaves are defined. We define the complexes
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
,
R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
,
R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
,
R\Gamma(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
in exactly the same way replacing the nearly
p
-adic representations with the versions for the Levi subgroups (i.e.,
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G})
,
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{H,n+1,\beta}^{\diamondsuit}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})
, etc.).

The cohomology complexes in Definition
4.4.4
carry an action of the Hecke algebra away from
p
, and also actions of
T^{G,-}
,
T^{H,-}
,
T^{H,+}
,
T^{H_{i},+}
respectively via the (analogous versions of the) normalised cohomological correspondences
\psi_{t}
in Definition
4.3.7
.

Convention 4.4.5
.

Suppose that we are in Situation
2.2.2
. From now on, we will often equip the complex
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
with the “unnormalised” action of
T^{G,-}
given by
(w_{1}^{-1}\star\mathbf{1})(t)\kappa_{G}^{*}(w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1})t\cdot-
, where
\cdot
denotes the action above. With this action, the natural map

R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,-)}

is
T^{G,-}
-equivariant. We also use similar conventions for the latter three complexes in Definition
4.4.4
(as well as the overconvergent versions) so that the natural maps between these complexes and the versions in Definition
4.4.1
are equivariant for the Hecke operators at
p
.

4.4.3.
Results from higher Coleman theory

In this section, we describe certain classicality results from higher Coleman theory which are relevant for the construction of the
p
-adic
L
-functions. We begin with the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4.6
.

Let
\kappa_{G}
be as in Situation
2.2.2
. Then the weights of
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
are

(4.4.7)

\bigcup_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\Sigma}\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in\{0,1,2\}\\
\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}=2\end{subarray}}\left\{(2i-t_{1}-\delta_{1},2j-t_{2}-\delta_{2};\xi):\begin{array}[]{c}i=0,\dots,t_{1}\\
j=0,\dots,t_{2}\\
i+j\leq\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}+\frac{r_{1}-r_{2}}{2}\\
2\xi=r_{1}+r_{2}+t_{1}+t_{2}-2(i+j-1)\end{array}\right\}

where
\Sigma
is the following set:

\Sigma=\left\{(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:\begin{array}[]{c}t_{1}+t_{2}\equiv r_{1}+r_{2}\text{ modulo }2\\
r_{1}-r_{2}\leq t_{1}+t_{2}\leq r_{1}+r_{2}\\
|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq r_{1}-r_{2}\end{array}\right\}.

Moreover, for any
\kappa
in the set of weights in (
4.4.7
), one has

v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t))\leq v_{p}(\kappa(t))

for any
t\in T^{G,-}
.

Proof.

The first part follows from
[
LSZ17
, Proposition 4.3.1]
. For the last claim, recall that
\kappa_{G}^{*}=(r_{1},-(r_{2}+2);r_{2}+1)
. It suffices to check the inequality for the following matrices

t_{\operatorname{S}}=\operatorname{diag}(1,1,p,p),\quad t_{\operatorname{Kl}}=\operatorname{diag}(1,p,p,p^{2})

since these matrices and the centre generate
T^{G,-}
. One can easily compute:

\displaystyle v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t_{\operatorname{S}}))=r_{2}+1,\quad

\displaystyle\quad v_{p}(\kappa(t_{\operatorname{S}}))=\frac{r_{1}+r_{2}}{2}+\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}-(i+j)+1,

\displaystyle v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t_{\operatorname{Kl}}))=r_{2},\quad
