\displaystyle\quad v_{p}(\kappa(t_{\operatorname{Kl}}))=r_{1}+r_{2}+t_{1}+\delta_{1}-2i.

The inequality
v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t_{\operatorname{S}}))\leq v_{p}(\kappa(t_{\operatorname{S}}))
follows from the inequality involving
i+j
in the definition (
4.4.7
). For the inequality
v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t_{\operatorname{Kl}}))\leq v_{p}(\kappa(t_{\operatorname{Kl}}))
, we note that the conditions in
\Sigma
imply that
t_{1}\leq r_{1}
, hence

r_{1}+r_{2}+t_{1}+\delta_{1}-2i\geq r_{1}+r_{2}-t_{1}\geq r_{2}

as required.
∎

Let
\kappa_{G}
be as in Situation
2.2.2
and recall the definition of
{{}^{M}W_{G}}
from §
4.1.2
. Let
\lambda\colon T^{G,-}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
be a monoid homomorphism. We say that
\lambda
satisfies
\mathrm{ss}_{w_{1}}^{M}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
if: for any
w\in{{}^{M}W_{G}}
with
w\neq w_{1}
, there exists an element
y\in T^{G,-}
such that
v_{p}(\lambda(y))<v_{p}((w^{-1}\star\kappa_{G}^{*})(y))
. We now state the first classicality result that we need.

Proposition 4.4.8
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer and
\kappa_{G}
as in Situation
2.2.2
. Consider the following morphisms

\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}\xleftarrow{\operatorname{res}}\operatorname{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{cores}}\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}.

where the first map is induced from restriction and the second map is induced from corestriction (and rigid GAGA). Then:

(1)

The above morphisms are
T^{G,-}
-equivariant, and for any
t\in T^{G,--}
and
h\in\mathbb{Q}
, all three cohomology groups admit slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to the action of
t
.

(2)

The right-hand map is surjective on finite-slope parts.

(3)

The left-hand map is an isomorphism on small slope
\mathrm{ss}_{w_{1}}^{M}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
parts.

Proof.

The fact that the morphisms are
T^{G,-}
-equivariant follows from the fact that they are built are from the same cohomological correspondence. Moreover, Lemma
4.4.2
and
[
BP
, §6.1.1]
imply that for
t\in T^{G,--}
:

•

The square of the operator corresponding to
t
acts compactly on
R\Gamma\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}
and hence this complex admits slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to the action of
t
for any
h\in\mathbb{Q}
. In particular, the finite slope part
R\Gamma\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-,\operatorname{fs})}
is quasi-isomorphic to the finite slope part of

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-}-\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-},[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G})\right).

•

The square of the operator corresponding to
t
acts compactly on

(4.4.9)

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G}))

and hence this complex admits slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to the action of
t
for any
h\in\mathbb{Q}
. In particular, the finite slope part of (
4.4.9
) is quasi-isomorphic to the finite slope part of

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G})\right).

Recall that
{{}^{M}W_{G}}=\{\operatorname{id},w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}\}
comprises of four elements, where the length of
w_{i}
is
i
. For brevity, set
\mathscr{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G})
. Consider the following filtration

\varnothing=\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{4}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{3}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{2}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{1}=\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(\overline{]X^{G}_{w_{2}}[}\right)\subset\mathcal{Z}^{-}_{0}=\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})

(c.f.,
[
BP21
, §5.5.2]
) which gives rise to the spectral sequence

E_{1}^{p,q}=\operatorname{H}^{p+q}_{\mathcal{Z}_{p}^{-}-\mathcal{Z}_{p+1}^{-}}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{p+1}^{-},\mathscr{F})^{\operatorname{fs}}\Rightarrow\operatorname{H}^{p+q}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),\mathscr{F})^{\operatorname{fs}}

where the finite-slope part is with respect to the action of
T^{G,-}
. By
[
BP
, Corollary 6.2.10]
, the conjectural lower bounds on slopes in
[
BP21
, Conjecture 5.9.2]
hold. This implies that: for any eigensystem
\lambda\colon T^{G,-}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
occurring in the cohomology
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-}}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),\mathscr{F})^{\operatorname{fs}}
, one has

v_{p}(\lambda(t))\geq v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t)).

for any
t\in T^{G,-}
. Indeed, it suffices to verify this for the sheaves associated with the graded pieces of
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
(which are
M_{G}
-representations); but by Lemma
4.4.6
, we know that the highest weight
\kappa
of any such graded piece satisfies
v_{p}(\kappa(t))\geq v_{p}(\kappa_{G}^{*}(t))
for any
t\in T^{G,-}
. We therefore see that
E_{1}^{3,q}
vanishes on small slope
\mathrm{ss}_{w_{1}}^{M}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
parts.

We have an exact triangle:

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),\mathscr{F}\right)\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),\mathscr{F}\right)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-}-\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{-},\mathscr{F}\right)\xrightarrow{+1}

hence the above vanishing implies that the restriction map is an isomorphism on small slope
\mathrm{ss}_{w_{1}}^{M}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
parts of cohomology. This proves part (3).

Finally we note that, since
\mathscr{F}
is coherent and vanishing along the boundary divisor, one has

\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-},\mathscr{F}\right)^{\operatorname{fs}}=0

for
i\geq 2
. Indeed, it suffices to show that
E_{1}^{0,q}
and
E_{1}^{1,q}
vanish on finite slope parts for
q\geq 2
, but this follows from
[
BP21
, Theorem 5.6.1]
. This implies that the map

\operatorname{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{-}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),\mathscr{F}\right)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{cores}}\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),\mathscr{F}\right)

is surjective on finite-slope parts and concludes the proof of part (2).
∎

We will also need a classicality result relating
p
-adic weight overconvergent cohomology with classical weight overconvergent cohomology. Let
\kappa_{G}
be as in Situation
2.2.2
. We say that
\lambda\colon T^{G,-}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
satisfies
\operatorname{ss}_{M,w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
if: for any
w\in W_{M_{G}}
with
w\neq\operatorname{id}
, there exists
y\in T^{G,-}
such that
v_{p}(\lambda(y))<v_{p}(((w_{1}^{-1}w)\star\kappa_{G}^{*})(y))
.

Proposition 4.4.10
.

Let
\kappa_{G}
be as in Situation
2.2.2
and
n\geq 1
any integer. Then the natural map

R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,-)}

is a quasi-isomorphism on small slope
\operatorname{ss}_{M,w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
parts (for the unnormalised action as in Convention
4.4.5
).

Proof.

This follows from the same proof as in
[
BP21
, Corollary 6.8.4]
in combination with
[
BP
, Corollary 6.2.16]
, which allows us to replace the “strictly small slope” condition with the small slope
\operatorname{ss}_{M,w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
condition.
∎

Finally, we will need to know when we can lift overconvergent cohomology classes to nearly overconvergent classes.

Proposition 4.4.11
.

Let
(A,A^{+})
be a complete Tate affinoid pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
n\geq 1
an integer. Let
\kappa_{G}\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to A^{\times}
be an
n
-analytic character. Set
S=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
.

(1)

For any
h\in\mathbb{Q}
,
t\in T^{G,--}
, and
x\in S(\mathbb{C}_{p})
, there exists a sufficiently small open quasi-compact affinoid neighbourhood
V=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})
of
x
in
S
such that both
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G,V}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
and
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G,V}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
admit slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to the action of
t
, where
\kappa_{G,V}
denotes the composition
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{G}}A^{\times}\to B^{\times}
.

(2)

Let
h\in\mathbb{Q}
and suppose that
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
and
R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
admit slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to the action of a fixed element
t\in T^{G,--}
. Then the natural map

(4.4.12)

\operatorname{H}^{2}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to\operatorname{H}^{2}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

induced from the map of representations (
2.3.5
) is surjective on slope
\leq h
parts.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from the fact that the action of
w_{1}tw_{1}^{-1}
on
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1,\beta}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}^{\square}_{G,n+1,\beta}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
is compact (and a similar argument as in
[
BP21
, §6.4.2]
). In particular, one can easily check that there exists an integer
m\geq 0
such that

R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G,V}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-),\leq h}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}\cap U}(U,[\mathcal{F}_{m}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})](-D_{G}))\right)^{\leq h}

where
\mathcal{F}_{m}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})=\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})/\operatorname{Fil}^{m+1}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})
and
\operatorname{Fil}^{m}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})
denotes the sub-representation of linear functionals
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n,\beta}^{\square,\circ}}(\kappa_{G,V})\to B
which vanish on the subspace of functions which are polynomial in the coordinates
a,b
from Lemma
2.3.2
of total degree
\leq m-1
(we set
\operatorname{Fil}^{0}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})=\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})
). In particular, we have
\mathcal{F}_{0}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})=\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{M}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})
.

By an inductive argument, it therefore suffices to show that

\left(\varinjlim_{U}\operatorname{H}^{3}_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}\cap U}(U,[\operatorname{Gr}^{j}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})](-D_{G}))\right)^{\leq h}=0

for any
0\leq j\leq m
, where
\operatorname{Gr}^{j}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})=\operatorname{Fil}^{j}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})/\operatorname{Fil}^{j+1}\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa^{*}_{G,V})=:M_{j}
. Note that
M_{j}
is a projective Banach
\mathcal{M}^{\square}_{G,n+1,\beta}
-representation over
B
. It suffices to show that

R\Gamma(\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}),[M_{j}](-D_{G}))

is concentrated in degree
0
for any open affinoid
\mathcal{U}\subset{]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}}K_{p}
(where
k\gg 1
is a sufficiently large integer so that
\mathcal{P}_{G,\operatorname{dR},n}
is defined over
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left({]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}}K_{p}\right)
). Indeed granted this, we can therefore follow the same strategy as in
[
BP21
, Theorem 5.6.1]
noting that
]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{k,k}\backslash]C^{G}_{w_{1}}[_{\bar{l},k}
(
l>k
) is covered by two acyclic open subspaces. But this claim about being concentrated in degree
0
follows from exactly the same proof as in
[
BP21
, Lemma 6.6.2]
.
∎

4.5.
p
-adic trilinear periods

We now define
p
-adic versions of
\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}
. Fix an integer
\beta\geq 1
throughout.

4.5.1.
Classical weight nearly overconvergent

Suppose that we are in Situation
2.2.2
. In what follows, we work with adic Shimura varieties (and their cohomology groups) over a fixed finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, but omit this from the notation. Recall that
\hat{\iota}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta})=\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}
and we have a natural map
[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G})\to\hat{\iota}_{*}[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{H}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{H})
induced from the branching law
\operatorname{br}^{\operatorname{nearly}}
from §
2.2.1
. This implies that we have a commutative diagram:

(4.5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}}(\mathcal{X}_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}](-D_{G}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{cores}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{cores}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}

Furthermore, we have an exterior cup product pairing:

(4.5.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H_{1}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{1}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}\otimes_{L}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H_{2}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{2}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}

\displaystyle(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}(\omega_{1})\smile\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{2}).

Let
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta}
denote the space of nearly overconvergent modular forms of level
K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}
as constructed in
[
GPR23
, Definition 2.3.1]
(for
g=\operatorname{id}
), i.e., one has
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta}=\varinjlim_{U}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(U,\mathcal{O}_{P_{H_{i},\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\right)
where
U
runs over all quasi-compact open subspaces of
P_{H_{i},\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
containing the closure of
\mathcal{IG}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}^{\operatorname{tor}}
. Then we have identifications:

\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}=\operatorname{Hom}_{T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\left(-\zeta_{H_{i}},\operatorname{Fil}_{t_{i}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta}\right)

where
\operatorname{Fil}_{\bullet}
denotes the filtration in
[
GPR23
, Theorem 2.3.2]
.

Definition 4.5.3
.

With notation as above, let
\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger}_{L}
denote the trilinear map given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{L}^{\ddagger}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{1}},\zeta_{H_{1}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{2}},\zeta_{H_{2}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}

\displaystyle\to L

\displaystyle(\eta,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle\hat{\iota}^{*}\eta,\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}\rangle

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the natural pairing between
\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}
and
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}
.

Remark 4.5.4
.

One has an overconvergent version of Definition
4.5.3
:

\mathcal{P}_{L}^{\dagger}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\dagger,-)}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{1}},\zeta_{H_{1}}\right)^{(\dagger,+)}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{2}},\zeta_{H_{2}}\right)^{(\dagger,+)}\to L

defined in exactly the same way (using the branching law from the bottom horizontal arrow of (
2.2.8
)). This is one of the trilinear pairings used in
[
LZ21a
]
. If

\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\dagger,+)}\subset\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}

and
\eta^{\ddagger}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}
, then one has the compatibility

\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger}_{L}(\eta^{\ddagger},\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}_{L}(\eta^{\dagger},\omega_{1},\omega_{2})

where
\eta^{\dagger}
denotes the image of
\eta^{\ddagger}
under the natural map
\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}\to\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\dagger,-)}
.

We note the following compatibility of the nearly overconvergent trilinear pairing with the algebraic version.

Lemma 4.5.5
.

Let
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}
be a class in the small slope
\operatorname{ss}^{M}_{w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
part, and let
\eta^{\operatorname{cl}}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}
denote the corresponding small slope
\operatorname{ss}^{M}_{w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
class via the morphisms in Proposition
4.4.8
. Let
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
and let
\operatorname{res}(\omega_{i})\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}})^{(\ddagger,+)}
denote its image under the restriction map. Then

\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger}_{L}(\eta,\operatorname{res}(\omega_{1}),\operatorname{res}(\omega_{2}))=\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{L}(\eta^{\operatorname{cl}},\omega_{1},\omega_{2}).

Proof.

This follows immediately from the commutative diagram (
4.5.1
).
∎

4.5.2.
p
-adic weight nearly overconvergent

Let
(A,A^{+})
be a complete Tate affinoid pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
n\geq 1
and let
\kappa_{G}\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to A^{\times}
be a
n
-analytic character. Let
\lambda\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to A^{\times}
be a
n
-analytic character and set
\kappa_{H}=w_{M_{G}}^{\operatorname{max}}\kappa_{G}+(\lambda,-\lambda;0)
. We can lift
\kappa_{H}-2\rho_{H}
to a pair of characters
(\zeta_{H_{1}},\zeta_{H_{2}})\colon T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to A^{\times}
via the identification
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Note that we have

\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\left(-\zeta_{H_{i}},\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta}\right)=:\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta,\zeta_{H_{i}}}

and
\varinjlim_{m\geq n}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,m\operatorname{-an},+)}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta,\zeta_{H_{i}}}
. By Remark
2.3.6
and the discussion following Remark
4.3.2
, we have an “
n
-analytic” version of the exterior cup product pairing in (
4.5.2
). We also have a natural pullback map

\hat{\iota}^{*}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

again via the branching law
\operatorname{br}^{n\operatorname{-an}}
in Proposition
2.3.7
and the discussion following Remark
4.3.2
.

Definition 4.5.6
.

With notation as above, let
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
denote the following
A
-trilinear pairing

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{1}},\zeta_{H_{1}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}\otimes\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{2}},\zeta_{H_{2}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}\to A

given by
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}(\eta,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\hat{\iota}^{*}\eta,\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}\rangle
. We let
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
denote the overconvergent version, defined in exactly the same way but replacing the cohomology groups with their “
\dagger
-versions”.

Remark 4.5.7
.

As above, the pairings
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
are compatible with each other by viewing
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
as nearly overconvergent classes, and considering the image of
\eta
under the map from nearly overconvergent cohomology to overconvergent cohomology. The pairing
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
appears in the construction of the
p
-adic
L
-function in
[
LZ21a
, Definition 10.6.2]
.

Remark 4.5.8
.

Since the
p
-adic branching laws in Remark
2.3.6
and Proposition
2.3.7
are compatible with changing
n
, the pairings
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
induce an
A
-trilinear pairing

\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,\operatorname{la}}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,\operatorname{la},-)}\otimes\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{1},\beta,\zeta_{H_{1}}}\otimes\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{2},\beta,\zeta_{H_{2}}}\to A

where
\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,\operatorname{la},-)}=\varprojlim_{m\geq n}\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,m\operatorname{-an},-)}
. A similar statement is true for
\mathcal{P}^{\dagger,\operatorname{la}}_{\lambda}
, and we note that
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
is independent of
n
and equal to the space
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta,\zeta_{H_{i}}}=\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H_{i},\beta,\zeta_{H_{i}}}
of weight
\zeta_{H_{i}}
, level
K^{H_{i}}_{\beta}
overconvergent modular forms.

Remark 4.5.9
.

It is an easy check to see that
\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}_{\lambda}
(and hence
\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger,\operatorname{la}}_{\lambda}
) is functorial in the pair
(A,A^{+})
.

We will also need the following compatibility with the classical weight nearly overconvergent pairing.

Lemma 4.5.10
.

Suppose that we are in Situation
2.2.2
, and set
(A,A^{+})=(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
and
\lambda=r_{1}+1-t_{1}
. Let
\eta^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
and let
\eta^{\ddagger}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-)}
denote its image under the natural map induced from
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{G,n+1,\beta}^{\square}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})\to\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
. For
i=1,2
, let
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,+)}\subset\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
. Then one has

\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}(\eta^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}},\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\mathcal{P}_{L}^{\ddagger}(\eta^{\ddagger},\omega_{1},\omega_{2}).

Proof.

This follows immediately from the compatibility of the branching laws
\operatorname{br}^{\operatorname{nearly}}
and
\operatorname{br}^{n\operatorname{-an}}
in Proposition
2.3.8
.
∎

4.5.3.
Trace compatibility

The final compatibility we will need is when
\beta\geq 1
varies. We first note that there are natural (unnormalised) trace maps

(4.5.11)

\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}\colon R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta+1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to R\Gamma(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

via the formalism in
[
BP21
, Lemma 2.1.2]
and using the natural map
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1,\beta+1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})\to\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1,\beta}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
. This latter map of representations is compatible with the branching law
\operatorname{br}^{n\operatorname{-an}}
and the analogous change-of-
\beta
map for
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\diamondsuit}_{H,n+1,\beta}}(\kappa_{H}^{*})
. Combining this with fact that both of the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1},\beta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id},\beta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1,\beta}}}

are Cartesian, implies that
\hat{\iota}^{*}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}=\operatorname{Tr}_{H,\beta}\circ\;\hat{\iota}^{*}
, where

\operatorname{Tr}_{H,\beta}\colon R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta+1},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to R\Gamma(K^{H}_{\beta},\kappa_{H}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

is the unnormalised trace map for
H
. We have the following compatibility:

Lemma 4.5.12
.

Suppose we are in the setting of §
4.5.2
. Let
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta+1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
and
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
for
i=1,2
. Then

\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}_{\lambda,\beta}(\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}(\eta),\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}_{\lambda,\beta+1}(\eta,\omega_{1},\omega_{2})

where we have added the subscript to denote the level we are working at and, for the right-hand side, we naturally view
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta+1}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
via pullback.
7
7

7

In fact,
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K_{\beta}^{H_{i}},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
is independent of
\beta
.

Proof.

This follows from the adjunction between
\operatorname{Tr}_{H,\beta}
and restriction (from level
K^{H}_{\beta}
to level
K^{H}_{\beta+1}
).
∎

Remark 4.5.13
.

We also note that if the target of (
4.5.11
) admits a slope
\leq h
decomposition with respect to some fixed
t\in T^{G,--}
, then so does the source of (
4.5.11
) (since the action of
t
factors through
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}
; c.f.,
[
BP21
, Lemma 4.2.14]
). Moreover, in this case, the trace map
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}
is then an isomorphism on slope
\leq h
parts. We will use this to lift small slope classes along the trace maps
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}
for any
\beta\geq 1
.

5.
Automorphic representations

We now introduce the automorphic representations of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}
and
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
that we will consider in this article.

5.1.
Automorphic representations of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}

Following
[
LSZ17
, §10.1]
, for the rest of the article we let
\pi
denote a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A})
which is:

•

non-CAP, and either non-endoscopic or of Yoshida-type;

•

discrete series at
\infty
;

•

globally generic.

Let
\pi=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}\pi_{v}
denote the restricted tensor product decomposition of
\pi
over all places of
\mathbb{Q}
. Then we can (and do) assume that
\pi
is cohomological of weight
\nu_{G}=(r_{1},r_{2};-(r_{1}+r_{2}))
with
r_{1}\geq r_{2}\geq 0
, i.e., the
(\mathfrak{g},K^{+}_{G,\infty})
-cohomology of
\pi_{\infty}\otimes V_{G}(\nu_{G})^{*}
is non-trivial. Here
K_{G,\infty}^{+}=\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}\cdot U_{2}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the standard maximal compact-mod-centre subgroup of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
. Note that the finite part
\pi_{f}
is definable over a number field (this is the “arithmetic normalisation” appearing in
[
LZ20
, Note 6.1.2]
).

Assumption 5.1.1
.

We assume that
\pi_{p}
is unramified. In particular, by the globally generic assumption,
\pi_{p}
is a generic unramified principal series representation of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Since
\pi
is globally generic, it is quasi-paramodular of level
(N,M)
in the sense of
[
Oka19
]
, where
M^{2}\mid N
,
M
is the conductor of the finite part of the central character of
\pi
, and
p\nmid N
by Assumption
5.1.1
. If we write the central character of
\pi
as
\widehat{\chi}\cdot|\!|s(-)|\!|^{-(r_{1}+r_{2})/2}
with
\chi
finite-order, then for brevity, we say that
\pi
has weight
\nu_{G}
, level
(N,M)
, and character
\chi
(where
M
is the conductor of
\chi
).

5.1.1.

Let
\ell
be a prime for which
\pi_{\ell}
is unramified. By the globally generic assumption, we can write
\pi_{\ell}=I_{B}^{G}(\theta_{\ell})
as a normalised induction from
B(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
to
G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
of a smooth unramified character
\theta_{\ell}\colon T(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Fix a Haar measure on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
such that
K_{\ell}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
has volume
1
, and let
\mathcal{H}_{\ell,R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{H}(K_{\ell}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})/K_{\ell},R)
denote the spherical Hecke algebra over a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
with convolution product with respect to the fixed Haar measure above.

Definition 5.1.2
.

We let
\Theta_{\pi,\ell}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{C}
denote the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra homomorphism through which
\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\mathbb{C}}
acts on
\pi_{\ell}^{G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})}
. For a finite set of places
S
containing
\infty
,
p
and all primes where
\pi
ramifies, we let

\Theta^{S}_{\pi}\colon\mathcal{H}^{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigotimes_{\ell\not\in S}\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{C}

denote the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra homomorphism given by
\Theta^{S}_{\pi}=\otimes_{\ell\notin S}\Theta_{\pi,\ell}
. This homomorphism is definable over a number field
E
, which will assume from now on and suppress from the notation.

5.1.2.

We now introduce certain Hecke operators at
p
. Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer and let
K_{\operatorname{Kl}}(p^{\beta})\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
K_{\operatorname{Si}}(p^{\beta})\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
) denote the compact open subgroup of elements which are of the form
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*&*&*\\
&*&*&*\\
&*&*&*\\
&&&*\end{smallmatrix}\right)
(resp.
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*&*&*\\
*&*&*&*\\
&&*&*\\
&&*&*\end{smallmatrix}\right)
) modulo
p^{\beta}
. Let
K_{\operatorname{B}}(p^{\beta})=K_{\operatorname{Si}}(p^{\beta})\cap K_{\operatorname{Kl}}(p^{\beta})
denote the upper-triangular Iwahori subgroup of depth
p^{\beta}
. We will often use the notation
K_{p,\operatorname{Si}}=K_{\operatorname{Si}}(p)
,
K_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}=K_{\operatorname{Kl}}(p)
,
K_{p,\operatorname{B}}=K_{\operatorname{B}}(p)
. For
\bullet\in\{\operatorname{Si},\operatorname{Kl},\operatorname{B}\}
we let
U_{p,\bullet}=U_{\bullet}(p)
and
U^{\prime}_{\bullet}(p^{\beta})
denote the following Hecke operators:

\displaystyle U_{p,\operatorname{Si}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\upsilon_{\operatorname{Si},1}^{-1}\operatorname{ch}\left(K_{p,\operatorname{Si}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&&&\\
&p&&\\
&&1&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{p,\operatorname{Si}}\right),

\displaystyle\quad U^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Si}}(p^{\beta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\upsilon_{\operatorname{Si},\beta}^{-1}\operatorname{ch}\left(K_{\operatorname{Si}}(p^{\beta})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
&1&&\\
&&p^{\beta}&\\
&&&p^{\beta}\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{\operatorname{Si}}(p^{\beta})\right)

\displaystyle U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\upsilon_{\operatorname{Kl},1}^{-1}\operatorname{ch}\left(K_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{2}&&&\\
&p&&\\
&&p&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}\right),

\displaystyle\quad U^{\prime}_{\operatorname{Kl}}(p^{\beta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\upsilon_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}^{-1}\operatorname{ch}\left(K_{\operatorname{Kl}}(p^{\beta})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
&p^{\beta}&&\\
&&p^{\beta}&\\
&&&p^{2\beta}\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{\operatorname{Kl}}(p^{\beta})\right)

\displaystyle U_{p,\operatorname{B}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\upsilon_{\operatorname{B},1}^{-1}\operatorname{ch}\left(K_{p,\operatorname{B}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{3}&&&\\
&p^{2}&&\\
&&p&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{p,\operatorname{B}}\right),

\displaystyle\quad U^{\prime}_{\operatorname{B}}(p^{\beta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\upsilon_{\operatorname{B},\beta}^{-1}\operatorname{ch}\left(K_{\operatorname{B}}(p^{\beta})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
&p^{\beta}&&\\
&&p^{2\beta}&\\
&&&p^{3\beta}\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{\operatorname{B}}(p^{\beta})\right)

where for brevity we have written
\upsilon_{\bullet,\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Vol}(K_{\bullet}(p^{\beta}))
. We will often simply write
U_{p,\bullet}^{\prime}=U^{\prime}_{\bullet}(p^{\beta})
when
\beta
is clear from the context. Furthermore, we will also write
U_{p,\operatorname{Si}},U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}
for the versions of the Hecke operators at Iwahori level (given by the same description replacing
\upsilon_{\bullet,1},K_{p,\bullet}
with
\upsilon_{\operatorname{B},1},K_{p,\operatorname{B}}
), and similarly for
U^{\prime}_{p,\operatorname{Si}},U^{\prime}_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}
at (depth
p^{\beta}
) Iwahori level.

For a
G
-dominant weight
\nu_{G}=(r_{1},r_{2};-(r_{1}+r_{2}))
, these operators naturally act on the singular cohomology groups
\operatorname{H}^{i}_{c}\left(Y_{G}(K^{p}K_{\operatorname{B}}(p^{\beta}))(\mathbb{C}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\right)
, where
\mathscr{V}_{\mathbb{Q}}
denotes the
\mathbb{Q}
-local system associated with the algebraic representation
V_{G}(\nu_{G})^{*}
. We let

U_{p,\operatorname{Si}}^{\circ}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U_{p,\operatorname{Si}},\quad U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}^{\circ}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p^{-r_{2}}U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}},\quad U_{p,B}^{\circ}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p^{-r_{2}}U_{p,B}

which are the optimal normalisations for the action of the Hecke operators on
\operatorname{H}^{i}_{c}\left(Y_{G}(K^{p}K_{\operatorname{B}}(p^{\beta}))(\mathbb{C}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathbb{Q}}\right)
(see
[
BP21
, Proposition 5.10.11]
).

More generally, recall that
T^{G,+}\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the submonoid of elements
t\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which satisfy
v_{p}(\alpha(t))\geq 0
for all positive roots
\alpha\in\Phi_{G}^{+}
. For any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
, we will consider the following (commutative) subalgebra

\displaystyle R[T^{G,+}]

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}\left(K_{p,\operatorname{B}}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/K_{p,\operatorname{B}},R\right)

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\mapsto\operatorname{Vol}(K_{p,\operatorname{B}})^{-1}\operatorname{ch}(K_{p,\operatorname{B}}\cdot t\cdot K_{p,\operatorname{B}}).

The following lemma describes the possible Hecke eigensystems at
p
that can occur in
\pi_{p}^{K_{p,\operatorname{B}}}
.

Lemma 5.1.3
.

Let
\pi
be as above and write
\pi_{p}=I_{B}^{G}(\theta_{p})
as a normalised induction of a smooth unramified character
\theta_{p}\colon T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Then the semisimplification of
\pi_{p}^{K_{p,\operatorname{B}}}
as a
\mathbb{C}[T^{G,+}]
-module can be described as

(5.1.4)

\bigoplus_{w\in W_{G}}\delta_{B}^{-1/2}(w\cdot\theta_{p})

where
\delta_{B}^{-1/2}(w\cdot\theta_{p})\colon T^{G,+}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denotes the character given by
\left[\delta_{B}^{-1/2}(w\cdot\theta_{p})\right](t)=\delta_{B}(t)^{-1/2}\theta_{p}(w^{-1}tw)
and
\delta_{B}
denotes the standard modulus character:

\delta_{B}(t_{1},t_{2},st_{2}^{-1},st_{1}^{-1})=|t_{1}|^{4}\cdot|t_{2}|^{2}\cdot|s|^{-3}.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Cas
, Theorem 3.3.3, Proposition 6.4.1]
.
∎

We will impose the following assumption.

Assumption 5.1.5
.

We assume that
\pi_{p}
is
p
-regular, i.e., the eigencharacters appearing in (
5.1.4
) are pairwise distinct (so, in particular,
\pi_{p}^{K_{p,\operatorname{B}}}
is a semisimple
\mathbb{C}[T^{G,+}]
-module).

5.1.3.

For convenience later on, we make the following definition.

Definition 5.1.6
.

By a
good
p
-stabilised
automorphic representation
\widetilde{\pi}=(\pi,\Theta_{\pi,p})
of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A})
, we mean the data of:

•

a cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A})
satisfying the assumptions at the start of §
5.1
, as well as Assumption
5.1.1
and Assumption
5.1.5
;

•

a character
\Theta_{\pi,p}\colon T^{G,+}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
which appears in the semisimple
\mathbb{C}[T^{G,+}]
-module
\pi_{p}^{K_{p,\mathrm{B}}}
.

By a
good
p
-stabilisation

\phi\in\widetilde{\pi}
, we mean a decomposible (non-zero) automorphic form

\phi=\phi_{\infty}\otimes\phi^{p}\otimes\phi_{p}\in\pi_{\infty}\otimes\left(\otimes_{\ell\neq p}\pi_{\ell}\right)^{K(N,M)^{p}}\otimes\pi_{p}^{K_{p,\operatorname{B}}}

where
\phi_{p}
is an eigenvector for the action of
T^{G,+}
with eigencharacter
\Theta_{\pi,p}
, and
\phi_{\infty}
lies in the (unique)
K_{G,\infty}^{+}
-type of highest weight
\kappa_{G}+(-1,-3;2)
. Here
K(N,M)^{p}\subset\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
denotes the quasi-paramodular subgroup of level
(N,M)
(with
N
equal to the conductor of
\pi
and
M
equal to the conductor of the finite-part of the central character of
\pi
).

It will also be useful to introduce a “normalised” version of these eigensystems. More precisely, given a good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation
\widetilde{\pi}
, we let
\Theta^{+}_{\pi,p}\colon T^{G,+}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denote the character satisfying

\Theta^{+}_{\pi,p}(t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\nu_{G}(t)\Theta_{\pi,p}(t),\quad\quad t\in T^{G,+},

where
\nu_{G}
is the weight of
\pi
.

We now introduce the relevant small slope conditions on good
p
-stabilised automorphic representations.

Definition 5.1.7
.

Let
\widetilde{\pi}=(\pi,\Theta_{\pi,p})
be a good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A})
. Via our fixed isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, we view
\Theta_{\pi,p}
as a character of
T^{G,+}
valued in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
. Let
v_{p}
denote the
p
-adic valuation on
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
, normalised so that
v_{p}(p)=1
. Then we say
\Theta_{\pi,p}
(or
\widetilde{\pi}
) is:

•

Klingen small slope
if

v_{p}(\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}^{\circ}))<1+r_{1}-r_{2};

•

Siegel small slope
if

v_{p}(\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Si}}^{\circ}))<1+r_{2};

•

Borel small slope
(or, simply,
small slope
) if
\Theta_{\pi,p}
is both Klingen and Siegel small slope.

We say
\Theta_{\pi,p}
(or
\widetilde{\pi}
) is Klingen (resp. Siegel, resp. Borel) ordinary if
v_{p}(\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}^{\circ}))=0
(resp.
v_{p}(\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Si}}^{\circ}))=0
, resp.
v_{p}(\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{B}}^{\circ}))=0
).

Remark 5.1.8
.

The above definition of (Borel) small slope is equivalent to the “
(+,\operatorname{ss}(\nu_{G}))
” small slope condition in
[
BP21
, Definition 5.11.2]
and is the usual requirement that appears in the theory of
p
-adic families of automorphic forms via Betti cohomology. Furthermore, if
\Theta_{\pi,p}
is small slope, then
w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\Theta_{\pi,p}
also satisfies the
\operatorname{ss}^{M}_{w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
and
\operatorname{ss}_{M,w_{1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
conditions from §
4.4.3
(with
\kappa_{G}=(r_{2}+2,-r_{1};-r_{2}-1)
); indeed, this follows from
[
BP21
, Proposition 5.11.10]
.

5.1.4.
Dual
p
-stabilisations

It will be useful to consider
p
-stabilisations for the transpose Hecke operators at
p
. Let
\phi\in\widetilde{\pi}
denote a good
p
-stabilisation in a good
p
-stabilised cuspidal automorphic representation of
G(\mathbb{A})
as above.

Definition 5.1.9
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
. We let
\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}\in\pi_{\infty}\otimes\pi_{f}^{K(N,M)^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}
denote the automorphic form given by

\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Theta_{\pi,p}(t_{p,\operatorname{B}})^{-\beta}[K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})s_{p,\operatorname{B}}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p)]\cdot\phi,\quad t_{p,\operatorname{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{3}&&&\\
&p^{2}&&\\
&&p&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\;s_{p,\operatorname{B}}=w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}t_{p,\operatorname{B}}(w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}})^{-1}.

This is an eigenvector for the action of
\bigotimes_{\ell\nmid Np}\mathcal{H}_{\ell,\mathbb{C}}
with eigencharacter
\Theta_{\pi}^{Np}=\otimes_{\ell\nmid Np}\Theta_{\pi,\ell}
, and an eigenvector for the action of
T^{G,-}
with eigencharacter
w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\Theta_{\pi,p}
. Furthermore, if
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}
denotes the (unnormalised) trace map from level
K(N,M)^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta+1})
to
K(N,M)^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, then
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}(\phi^{\prime}_{\beta+1})=\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

Conversely, given any trace compatible system of eigenvectors
\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}
as above, one can recover the good
p
-stabilisation
\phi
by reversing this process. Hence
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}
encode the same information.

5.2.
Automorphic representations of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}

We now introduce the cuspidal automorphic representations of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
that we will consider.

5.2.1.

Let
N\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
be integers. Let
f
be a normalised cuspidal new eigenform of weight
1+t
, level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
, and nebentypus
\chi
. We impose the following assumption:

Assumption 5.2.1
.

We assume that
p\nmid N
. Furthermore, we assume that:

(1)

(
p
-regularity) The roots of the Hecke polynomial
X^{2}-a_{p}(f)X+p^{t}\chi(p)
are distinct.

(2)

If
t\geq 1
and
\rho_{f}\colon\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
denotes the two-dimensional
p
-adic Galois representation associated with
f
, then
\rho_{f}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
does not split as a direct sum of two characters (note that this is automatic if
v_{p}(a_{p}(f))\neq 0
).

(3)

If
t=0
, then
f
does not have real multiplication by a quadratic field in which
p
splits (in the sense of
[
LZ16
, Notation 4.7.1]
).

Let
\sigma
denote the cuspidal automorphic representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
associated with
f
with central character
|\!|\operatorname{det}(-)|\!|^{(1+t)/2}\widehat{\chi}
, where
\chi
is a Dirichlet character. For brevity, we say that
\sigma
has weight
1+t
, level
N
, and character
\chi
.

Definition 5.2.2
.

By a
good
p
-stabilised
automorphic representation
\widetilde{\sigma}=(\sigma,\alpha)
of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
, we mean the data of:

•

a cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of weight
1+t
, level
N
, and character
\chi
as above, satisfying Assumption
5.2.1
;

•

a choice of root
\alpha
of the Hecke polynomial in Assumption
5.2.1
(1).

By the
good
p
-stabilisation

\phi\in\widetilde{\sigma}
, we mean the automorphic form
\phi\in\sigma
associated with the (normalised)
p
-stabilisation
f_{\alpha}(-)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f(-)-p^{t}\chi(p)\alpha^{-1}f(p\cdot-)
; explicitly, the automorphic form
\phi\colon\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})\to\mathbb{C}
is described as:

\phi(\gamma g_{\infty}k)=(\operatorname{det}g_{\infty})^{1+t}(ci+d)^{-(1+t)}f_{\alpha}\left(\frac{ai+b}{ci+d}\right),\quad\quad\gamma\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}),\;g_{\infty}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})_{+},\;k\in K_{1}(N),

where
K_{1}(N)\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})
denotes the adelic version of the arithmetic subgroup
\Gamma_{1}(N)
.

5.3.
Set-up and tame test data

As in
[
LZ21a
, §10.2]
, we fix the following data in the two situations which will correspond to the
p
-adic
L
-functions for
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}
and
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}
respectively.

5.3.1.
Case A

Let
(N_{0},M_{0})
be positive integers prime to
p
with
M_{0}^{2}\mid N_{0}
, and let
\chi_{0}
be a Dirichlet character of conductor
M_{0}
. Let
(N_{2},M_{2})
be positive integers prime to
p
with
M_{2}\mid N_{2}
, and let
\chi_{2}
be a Dirichlet character of conductor
M_{2}
. Let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
denote a finite extension containing
\mu_{N_{0}}
and
\mu_{N_{2}}
, and such that
\chi_{0},\chi_{2}
are valued in
L
.

Let
S
denote the set of primes dividing
N_{0}N_{2}
. As in
[
LZ21a
, §10.2]
, we consider the following tame test data
\gamma_{S}=(\gamma_{0,S},\Phi_{S})
, where:
\gamma_{0,S}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{S})
and
\Phi_{S}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{S}^{\oplus 2},L)
is a Schwartz function lying in the
(\widehat{\chi}_{0}\widehat{\chi}_{2})^{-1}
-eigenspace for the diagonal action of
\mathbb{Z}_{S}^{\times}
.

Let
T
denote an auxiliary finite set of primes which is disjoint from
S\cup\{p\}
. Let
K_{S}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{S})
denote the quasi-paramodular subgroup of level
(N_{0},M_{0})
. We consider compact open subgroups
\widehat{K}_{S}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{S})
,
K_{T}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})
satisfying the following conditions:

•

K_{T}
is a normal subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})
;

•

\widehat{K}_{S}\subset\gamma_{0,S}K_{S}\gamma_{0,S}^{-1}
;

•

the projection of
\widehat{K}_{S}\cap H
to the first factor acts trivially on
\Phi_{S}
;

•

the projection of
\widehat{K}_{S}\cap H
to the second factor is contained in
\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ mod }N_{2}\right\}
;

•

\mathcal{K}=K_{T}\prod_{\ell\not\in T}G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
\mathcal{K}\cap H
and the projections of
\mathcal{K}\cap H
to each factor are neat compact open subgroups.

One can easily see that such compact open subgroups exist (for example, one can take
T
to have least three elements and
K_{T}
is the subgroup of elements which are congruent to the identity modulo the primes in
T
). With the choice of these compact open subgroups, we set

\displaystyle K^{p}=K_{S}\cdot K_{T}\cdot\prod_{\ell\notin S\cup T\cup\{p\}}G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\quad\subset\quad G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})

\displaystyle\widehat{K}^{p}=\widehat{K}_{S}\cdot K_{T}\cdot\prod_{\ell\notin S\cup T\cup\{p\}}G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\quad\subset\quad G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}).

As in §
3.1.1
, we define
K^{G}_{\beta}
,
\widehat{K}^{G}_{\beta}
etc. with respect to these choices of
K^{p}
and
\widehat{K}^{p}
.

5.3.2.
Case B

In this case, we let
(N_{0},M_{0},\chi_{0})
and
(N_{2},M_{2},\chi_{2})
as above. We let
(N_{1},M_{2})
be positive integers prime to
p
with
M_{1}\mid N_{1}
, and take
\chi_{1}
to be a Dirichlet character of conductor
M_{1}
. Let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension containing
\mu_{N_{i}}
(
i=0,1,2
) and such that
\chi_{0},\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
are valued in
L
. We let
S
denote the set of primes dividing
N_{0}N_{1}N_{2}
and let
T
denote an auxiliary set of primes disjoint from
S\cup\{p\}
.

In this case, we assume that
\chi_{0}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}=1
and we fix elements
\gamma_{S}=(\gamma_{0,S},\gamma_{1,S})\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{S})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{S})
. As above, we let
\widehat{K}_{S}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{S})
and
K_{T}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})
be compact open subgroups satisfying all the bullet points in §
5.3.1
, except the third one. Instead, we replace the third bullet point with the condition:

•

the projection of
\widehat{K}_{S}\cap H
to the first factor is contained in
\gamma_{1,S}\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ mod }N_{1}\right\}\gamma_{1,S}^{-1}
.

We define
K^{p}
and
\widehat{K}^{p}
in exactly the same way as in §
5.3.1
.

5.4.
L
-functions

We recall the
L
-functions we are interested in studying.

5.4.1.

Suppose that we are in Case A (§
5.3.1
) and Situation
2.2.2
. Let
\widetilde{\pi}=(\pi,\Theta_{\pi,p})
be a good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation of
G(\mathbb{A})
of weight
\nu_{G}
, level
(N_{0},M_{0})
and character
\chi_{0}
. Let
\widetilde{\sigma}=(\sigma,\alpha)
be a good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
of weight
1+t_{2}
, level
N_{2}
, and character
\chi_{2}
. Let
\Pi=\pi\otimes|\!|s(-)|\!|^{(r_{1}+r_{2})/2}
and
\Sigma=\sigma\otimes|\!|\operatorname{det}(-)|\!|^{-(1+t_{2})/2}
denote the unitary normalisations of
\pi
and
\sigma
respectively.

Definition 5.4.1
.

Let
\chi
be a Dirichlet character of
p
-th power conductor.

(1)

Let
L(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)
denote the
L
-function associated with the automorphic representation
\left(\Pi\otimes\widehat{\chi}\right)\boxtimes\Sigma
of
G(\mathbb{A})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
with respect to the
8
-dimensional representation of
\widehat{G}(\mathbb{C})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
given by the tensor product of the
4
-dimensional spin representation of
\widehat{G}(\mathbb{C})
and the
2
-dimensional standard representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. In particular, if
\ell\not\in S\cup\{p\}
and
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3},\theta_{4})
(resp.
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
) denotes the Satake parameters
8
8

8

In particular, the eigenvalues of
U_{\ell,\operatorname{Si}}
on
\Pi_{\ell}^{K_{\ell,\operatorname{B}}}
are given by
\{\ell^{3/2}\theta_{1},\ell^{3/2}\theta_{2},\ell^{3/2}\theta_{3},\ell^{3/2}\theta_{4}\}
.
of
\Pi_{\ell}
(resp.
\Sigma_{\ell}
) via the identification
\widehat{G}\cong\operatorname{GSp}_{4}
, then the local
L
-factor at
\ell
is described as:

L_{\ell}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)=L(\Pi_{\ell}\times\Sigma_{\ell},\chi_{\ell},s)=\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{j=1}^{2}\left(1-\chi(\ell)\theta_{i}\gamma_{j}\ell^{-s}\right)^{-1}.

(2)

Let
\Lambda(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)=L_{\infty}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)L(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)
denote the completed
L
-function, and set
w=r_{1}+r_{2}+t_{2}+3
. Explicitly, the archimedean
L
-factor is given by

L_{\infty}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+w/2-r_{2}-t_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+w/2-r_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+w/2-t_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+w/2)

where
\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)
(and
\Gamma(-)
is the usual Gamma function). Furthermore, the completed
L
-function satisfies the following functional equation:

\Lambda(\Pi\times\Sigma,(\chi\chi_{0}\chi_{2})^{-1},1-s)=\varepsilon(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)\Lambda(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)

where
\varepsilon(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)
is entire and nowhere vanishing.

(3)

The critical values of the
L
-function
L(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)
are of the form
s=j-w/2
, where
r_{2}+t_{2}+2\leq j\leq r_{1}+2
.

(4)

We define the modified
L
-factor at
p
to be:

\mathcal{E}_{p}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\prod_{i,j=1}^{2}\frac{1-p^{s-1}\theta_{i}^{-1}\gamma_{j}^{-1}}{1-p^{-s}\theta_{i}\gamma_{j}}&\text{ if }\chi=1\\
G(\chi)^{-4}p^{4cs}\left(\theta_{1}\theta_{2}\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}\right)^{-2c}&\text{ if }\operatorname{cond}(\chi)=p^{c}\text{ with }c\geq 1\end{array}\right.

where
\{\theta_{i}:i=1,\dots,4\}
are ordered so that
\Theta_{\Pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}})=p^{2}\theta_{1}\theta_{2}
.

For any set
S^{\prime}
of places of
\mathbb{Q}
, we let
\Lambda^{S^{\prime}}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi,s)
denote the
L
-function with Euler factors at places in
S^{\prime}
removed.

Since
\Pi
is globally generic, we can consider its associated Whittaker model (with respect to the standard additive character of
\mathbb{A}/\mathbb{Q}
) as in
[
LPSZ21
, §9.1]
. We can (and do) assume that this Whittaker model decomposes as a product of local Whittaker models satisfying the conditions in
loc.cit.
. We make similar assumptions on the local Whittaker models for
\Sigma
. Let
v
be a place of
\mathbb{Q}
and let
\varphi_{v}\in\Pi_{v}
,
\lambda_{v}\in\Sigma_{v}
,
\Phi_{v}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{v}^{\oplus 2})
, and
\chi_{v}\colon\mathbb{Q}_{v}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
a smooth unitary character. Then, as in
[
LPSZ21
, Definition 8.3]
, we can consider the local zeta integral:

Z(\varphi_{v},\lambda_{v},\Phi_{v};\chi_{v},s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})N_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{v})\backslash H(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}W_{\varphi_{v}}(h)f^{\Phi_{v}}(h_{1};\chi_{v}^{2}\chi_{0,v}\chi_{2,v},s)W_{\lambda_{v}}(h_{2})\chi_{v}(\operatorname{det}h_{2})dh

where
W_{\varphi_{v}}
(resp.
W_{\lambda_{v}}
) denotes the image of
\varphi_{v}
(resp.
\lambda_{v}
) under the local Whittaker model of
\Pi_{v}
(resp.
\Sigma_{v}
), and
f^{\Phi_{v}}
is the Siegel section defined in
[
LPSZ21
, §8.1]
. If
v
is non-archimedean, then the Haar measure
dh
is normalised so that the volume of
H(\mathbb{Z}_{v})
is
1
.

We consider the following data:

•

For any
\beta\geq 1
, let

\eta_{\pi,\beta}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)_{\mathbb{C}}^{G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}

be a generator of the one-dimensional eigenspace under the action of the Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}^{S_{0}}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[T^{G,-}]
with eigencharacter
\Theta_{\pi}^{S_{0}}\otimes\left(w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\Theta_{\pi,p}\right)
, where
S_{0}
denotes the set of places dividing
\infty N_{0}p
. This eigenspace is indeed one-dimensional by the arguments in
[
LZ21a
, Proposition 2.7.2]
. More precisely, by the work of Su
[
Su19
]
, if we localise this cohomology group at the kernel
I
of the above eigencharacter, then
\pi
is the only automorphic representation that appears in this localised cohomology group (if
\pi^{\prime}
appears in this localised cohomology group, then it must be globally generic and locally isomorphic to
\pi
away from
S_{0}
– this implies
\pi^{\prime}\cong\pi

[
Sou87
]
). We see that

\operatorname{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)_{\mathbb{C},I}^{G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}=m(\pi)\operatorname{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\left(\otimes_{\ell\neq p}\pi_{\ell}\right)^{K(N_{0},M_{0})^{p}}\otimes\pi_{p}^{K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}[w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\Theta_{\pi,p}]\right)

where
[\cdots]
denotes the generalised eigenspace and
m(\pi)
is the multiplicity of
\pi
in the discrete spectrum. Since
m(\pi)=1
(see
[
JS07
]
),
\operatorname{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}\pi_{p}^{K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}[w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\Theta_{\pi,p}]=\operatorname{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}\pi_{p}^{K_{p,\operatorname{B}}}[\Theta_{\pi,p}]=1
(see §
5.1.4
), and
K(N_{0},M_{0})
is the quasi-paramodular group of level
(N_{0},M_{0})
, we see that this localised cohomology group is one-dimensional.

We can (and do) assume that
\eta_{\pi,\beta}
is defined over the field of definition of
\pi
(which is a number field). Furthermore, if we view
\eta_{\pi,\beta}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{G,\infty}^{+}}(\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G}),\mathscr{A}_{0}(G)^{K^{G}_{\beta}})
via
[
Su19
]
, then we note that
\eta_{\pi,\beta}(v)=\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}
for some good
p
-stabilisation
\phi
in
\widetilde{\pi}
for any
v\in\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G})
(if non-zero, which is equivalent to the image of
v
under the map
\wedge^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G})\to V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G}+(-1,-3;2))
being non-zero).

•

Suppose that
\xi_{2}=0
(with notation as in Situation
2.2.2
). Let

\omega_{\sigma,\beta}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\widehat{K}^{H_{2}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{2}}\right)_{\mathbb{C}}

denote (the restiction to level
\widehat{K}^{H_{2}}_{\beta}
of) the cohomology class corresponding to the good
p
-stabilisation in
\widetilde{\sigma}
. This cohomology class is defined over the field obtained by adjoining
\mu_{N_{2}}
to the field of definition of
\sigma
.
9
9

9

One can normalise
\omega_{\sigma,\beta}
further by the Gauss sum of
\chi_{2}^{-1}
so that the class is defined over the field of definition of
\sigma
, however we will not consider this class in this article. See
[
LZ21a
, §10.5]
for more details.

•

Let
\chi
be a Dirichlet character of
p
-th power conductor, and let
0\leq j\leq r_{1}-r_{2}-t_{2}
. Let
\Phi^{(p)}
denote the Schwartz function on
(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})^{\oplus 2}
given by
\Phi_{S}\otimes\bigotimes_{\ell\not\in S}\operatorname{ch}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}^{\oplus 2})
. For
\beta\geq\operatorname{max}(2\operatorname{max}(1,c(\chi)),\operatorname{max}(1,c(\chi))+1)
, we let

\omega_{\operatorname{Eis},j,\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi_{1}}(d-j-\chi,j+\chi)\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\widehat{K}^{H_{1}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{1}}\right)_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{nearly}},\quad d=r_{1}-r_{2}-t_{2},

denote the Eisenstein class as in §
6.1.1
. This is defined over a number field.

For brevity, set
\mathscr{F}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\left(\mathbb{I}_{\overline{P}_{G}}(\kappa_{G})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\right)^{*}
. Consider the diagram:

\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}\xrightarrow{f_{2}^{\vee}}\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(X_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta}),[\mathscr{F}](-D_{G})\right)\xleftarrow{f_{1}^{\vee}}\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right).

where
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are the morphisms in §
2.2.2
. As explained in
[
LPSZ21
, Proposition 6.3]
, these morphisms become isomorphisms after localising at the kernel of
\Theta_{\pi}^{S\cup T\cup\{p\}}
(recall that
S
is the set of primes dividing
N_{0}N_{2}
). We let
\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}
denote the unique lift of
\eta_{\pi,\beta}
lying in the (generalised) eigenspace for
\Theta_{\pi}^{S\cup T\cup\{p\}}
. By our assumptions, one has a natural map
X_{G}(\widehat{K}^{G}_{\beta})\to X_{G}(K^{G}_{\beta})
induced from right-translation by
\gamma_{0,S}
, and we consider the class

\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(\widehat{K}^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly}}

obtained as the pullback of
\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta}
under this morphism. Finally, we consider the following product of local zeta integrals and
L
-factors:

\mathcal{Z}_{S}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\gamma_{S};\chi_{S},s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\prod_{\ell\in S}\frac{Z(\gamma_{0,\ell}\cdot\varphi_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell},\Phi_{\ell};\chi_{\ell},s)}{L(\Pi_{\ell}\times\Sigma_{\ell},\chi_{\ell},s)}

for (non-zero)
\varphi_{S}=\otimes_{\ell\in S}\varphi_{\ell}\in\bigotimes_{\ell\in S}\Pi_{\ell}
,
\lambda_{S}=\otimes_{\ell\in S}\lambda_{\ell}\in\bigotimes_{\ell\in S}\Sigma_{\ell}
, and
\chi_{S}\colon\mathbb{Q}_{S}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
a smooth unitary character.

Remark 5.4.2
.

For any
\ell\in S
, one can show that the local zeta integrals
Z(\varphi_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell},\Phi_{\ell};\chi_{\ell},s)
as
(\varphi_{\ell},\lambda_{\ell},\Phi_{\ell})
vary form a fractional ideal of
\mathbb{C}[\ell^{\pm s}]
containing the constant functions (see
[
LPSZ21
, Theorem 8.9]
). It is known in many cases that this fractional ideal is generated by
L(\Pi_{\ell}\times\Sigma_{\ell},\chi_{\ell},s)
(see
[
Loe24
]
), although the general case is still open. For example, suppose that
\chi_{S}
is unramified. Then in any of the following (non-exhaustive list of) cases:

•

the central character of
\Pi
is a square;

•

\Sigma
is not supercuspidal at any prime in
S
;

•

the integers
N_{0}
and
N_{2}
are coprime;

one can always find a finite linear combination of
\mathcal{Z}_{S}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\gamma_{S};\chi_{S},s)
(for different choices of
\gamma_{S}
) such that this linear combination is equal to the constant function
1
in the variable
s\in\mathbb{C}
.

The following proposition describes the archimedean period
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
in terms of the
L
-function associated with
\Pi\times\Sigma
.

Proposition 5.4.3
.

Let
\chi
be a Dirichlet character of
p
-th power conductor, and let
0\leq j\leq r_{1}-r_{2}-t_{2}
. Let
\beta\geq\operatorname{max}(2\operatorname{max}(1,c(\chi)),\operatorname{max}(1,c(\chi))+1)
. Then there exists a scalar
\Omega_{\pi}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, independent of
\chi
,
j
,
\beta
, and
\gamma_{S}
, such that

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{alg}}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta},\omega_{\operatorname{Eis},j,\beta},\omega_{\sigma,\beta})=\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma_{S},j,\chi^{-1}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{p}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi^{-1},j+(1-d)/2)\cdot\frac{\Lambda^{\{p\}}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi^{-1},j+(1-d)/2)}{\Omega_{\pi}}

where
\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma_{S},j,\chi^{-1}}=\mathcal{Z}_{S}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\gamma_{S};\widehat{\chi}_{S}^{-1},j+(1-d)/2)
with
\varphi=\phi\otimes|\!|s(-)|\!|^{(r_{1}+r_{2})/2}
and
\lambda=\psi\otimes|\!|\operatorname{det}(-)|\!|^{-(1+t_{2})/2}
for:

•

\phi
equal to the unique good
p
-stabilisation in
\widetilde{\pi}
such that
\eta_{\pi,\beta}((\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2})\otimes\jmath_{M_{G}}(w_{\kappa_{H}}))=\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}
with notation as in Proposition
3.3.4
;

•

\psi
equal to the good
p
-stabilisation in
\widetilde{\sigma}
.

Proof.

Throughout the course of this proof, we write
\doteq
to mean equal up to a non-zero element of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
independent of
\chi
,
j
,
\beta
, and
\gamma_{S}
. By
[
LPSZ21
, Lemma 6.5]
, the hypotheses of Proposition
3.3.4
are satisfied. Therefore, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{alg}}

\displaystyle(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta},\omega_{\operatorname{Eis},j,\beta},\omega_{\sigma,\beta})

\displaystyle=(2\pi i)^{-2}\operatorname{Vol}(\mathcal{K}_{\beta};dh)^{-1}\int_{[H]}(\gamma_{0,S}\hat{\gamma}\cdot\phi^{\prime}_{\beta})(h)|\!|\operatorname{det}h_{1}|\!|^{\tfrac{1+d}{2}-\xi_{1}}\widehat{\chi}(\operatorname{det}h_{1})^{-1}E^{\Phi}(h_{1};\widehat{\chi},j-\tfrac{d-1}{2})\psi(h_{2})dh

\displaystyle\doteq p^{4\beta}\int_{Z_{G}(\mathbb{A})H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})}(\gamma_{0,S}\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta,\chi}^{\prime})(h)E^{\Phi}(h_{1};\widehat{\chi},j-\tfrac{d-1}{2})\lambda(h_{2})dh\;=:\;A

where
\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta,\chi}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\phi^{\prime}_{\beta}\otimes(|\!|-|\!|^{(r_{1}+r_{2})/2}\widehat{\chi}^{-1}\circ s)\in\Pi\otimes\widehat{\chi}^{-1}
, the Schwartz function is
\Phi=\Phi^{(d+1)}_{\infty}\Phi^{(p)}\Phi_{p,\chi^{-1},\chi}
(with notation as in
[
LPSZ21
, Definition 7.5, §8.5.1]
), and we have used the fact that the argument is invariant under the action of
Z_{G}(\mathbb{A})
. Applying
[
LPSZ21
, §8.5, Proposition 9.3(ii)]
, we see that

A\doteq\Lambda^{\{p\}}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\chi^{-1},j+(1-d)/2)\cdot\mathcal{Z}_{S}(\Pi\times\Sigma,\gamma_{S};\widehat{\chi}_{S}^{-1},j+(1-d)/2)\cdot\left(p^{4\beta}Z(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta,p},\lambda_{p},\Phi_{p,\chi^{-1},\chi};\widehat{\chi}^{-1}_{p},j+(1-d)/2)\right).

The proposition now follows from Corollary
A.0.4
(note that
\widehat{\chi}^{-1}_{p}|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}=\chi
).
∎

5.4.2.

Suppose that we are in Case B (§
5.3.2
) and Situation
2.2.2
. Let
d_{2}=t_{2}
and let
0\leq d_{1}\leq t_{1}
be any choice of integer. Let
\widetilde{\pi}=(\pi,\Theta_{\pi,p})
be a good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation of
G(\mathbb{A})
of weight
\nu_{G}
, level
(N_{0},M_{0})
and character
\chi_{0}
. For
i=1,2
, let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}=(\sigma_{i},\alpha_{i})
be good
p
-stabilised automorphic representations of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
of weight
1+d_{i}
, level
N_{i}
, and character
\chi_{i}
. Let
\Pi=\pi\otimes|\!|s(-)|\!|^{(r_{1}+r_{2})/2}
and
\Sigma_{i}=\sigma_{i}\otimes|\!|\operatorname{det}(-)|\!|^{-(1+d_{i})/2}
denote the unitary normalisations of
\pi
and
\sigma_{i}
respectively.

Remark 5.4.4
.

Note that the assumption
\chi_{0}\cdot\chi_{1}\cdot\chi_{2}=1
in §
5.3.2
implies that
d_{1}+d_{2}\equiv r_{1}-r_{2}
modulo
2
(and hence
d_{1}\equiv t_{1}
modulo
2
).

In this setting, we are interested in the “triple-product”
L
-function
L(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},s)
associated with the automorphic representation
\Pi\boxtimes\Sigma_{1}\boxtimes\Sigma_{2}
of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
with respect to the
16
-dimensional representation of the dual group given by the tensor product of the
4
-dimensional spin representation and the
2
-dimensional standard representations for each
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
-factor. The central critical value of this
L
-function is at
s=1/2
. Under certain hypotheses on these automorphic representations, the Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture for general spin groups
[
Emo20
]
predicts a relation between the central critical
L
-value
L(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},1/2)
and the automorphic period

\mathscr{P}(\varphi,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{H(\mathbb{Q})Z_{G}(\mathbb{A})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})}\varphi(h)\lambda_{1}(h_{1})\lambda_{2}(h_{2})dh

for
\varphi\in\Pi
and
\lambda_{i}\in\Sigma_{i}
. This conjecture is known when
\Pi
is of Yoshida or twisted Yoshida type, but is open in general. Nevertheless, it is natural to study the
p
-adic interpolation of the automorphic periods
\mathscr{P}
in place of the central critical
L
-values.

In fact, we are interested in these automorphic periods for specific choices of input data. More precisely, let
\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}}=\otimes_{v}\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}}_{v}\in\Pi=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}\Pi_{v}
, with:

•

\varphi_{\infty}^{\operatorname{sph}}\in\Pi_{\infty}
is the (unique up to scalar) vector obtained as the image of
(\alpha_{1}\wedge\alpha_{2})\odot\jmath_{M_{G}}(w_{\kappa_{H}})
under the (unique up to scalar)
K_{G,\infty}^{\circ}
-equivariant homomorphism
V_{M_{G}}(\kappa_{G}-2\rho_{G,\operatorname{nc}})\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}
. Here
K_{G,\infty}^{\circ}\subset K_{G,\infty}
is the maximal compact subgroup,
\odot
denotes the Cartan product, and
\rho_{G,\operatorname{nc}}
is the half-sum of positive non-compact roots.

•

For
\ell
finite,
\varphi_{\ell}^{\operatorname{sph}}\in\Pi_{\ell}
is the Whittaker new vector. In particular
\varphi_{p}^{\operatorname{sph}}
is the spherical vector.

We also let
\lambda_{i}^{\operatorname{sph}}\in\Sigma_{i}
denote the automorphic form associated with the holomorphic new eigenform associated with
\Sigma_{i}
(so
\lambda_{i}^{\operatorname{sph}}
lies in the minimal
K_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty}^{+}
-type at
\infty
and is the Whittaker new vector at finite primes. Let
\delta
denote the operator on (the
K_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty}^{+}
-finite vectors in)
\Sigma_{1,\infty}
given by the action of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathfrak{gl}_{2,\mathbb{C}}
(the Maass–Shimura differential operator).

We have the following result:

Lemma 5.4.5
.

Suppose that the local signs of
L(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},s)
at all finite places are equal to
+1
, and suppose that the local and global Gan–Gross–Prasad conjectures for general spin groups hold (as described in
[
LZ21b
, §2]
). Then
L(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},1/2)\neq 0
if and only if there exists a choice of
\gamma_{S}=(\gamma_{0,S},\gamma_{1,S})\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{S})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{S})
such that
\mathscr{P}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\delta^{(t_{1}-d_{1})/2}\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{sph}},\lambda_{2}^{\operatorname{sph}})\neq 0
.

Proof.

Note that the conditions in Situation
2.2.2
(which place us in region (f) of
[
LZ21b
, Figure 2]
) imply that the local sign at
\infty
is
+1
. Fix bases
\mathfrak{z}_{v}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{H(\mathbb{Q}_{v})}(\Pi_{v}\boxtimes\Sigma_{1,v}\boxtimes\Sigma_{2,v},\mathbb{C})
(which exist by the local Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture). The “if direction” of the lemma follows immediately from the global Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture, so from now on we will assume that
L(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},1/2)\neq 0
. In particular, this implies that the automorphic period
\mathscr{P}(\cdots)
is not identically zero on
\Pi\boxtimes\Sigma_{1}\boxtimes\Sigma_{2}
.

This implies that there exist decomposable
\psi\in\Pi
,
\mu_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}
,
\mu_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}
such that
\mathscr{P}(\psi,\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\neq 0
. In particular, we must have
\mathfrak{z}_{v}(\psi_{v},\mu_{1,v},\mu_{2,v})\neq 0
. By local multiplicity one, we have

\mathscr{P}(\psi^{\prime},\mu_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}_{2})=\prod_{v}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{v}(\psi^{\prime}_{v},\mu_{1,v}^{\prime},\mu_{2,v}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{z}_{v}(\psi_{v},\mu_{1,v},\mu_{2,v})}\right)\cdot\mathscr{P}(\psi,\mu_{1},\mu_{2})

for any decomposable
\psi^{\prime}\in\Pi
,
\mu_{i}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{i}
(note that almost all terms in the product are
1
). We note that:

•

\mathfrak{z}_{\infty}(\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}}_{\infty},\delta^{(t_{1}-d_{1})/2}\lambda_{1,\infty}^{\operatorname{sph}},\lambda_{2,\infty}^{\operatorname{sph}})\neq 0
by the discussion in
[
LPSZ21
, §8.5]
;

•

\xi_{\ell}(\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}}_{\ell},\lambda_{1,\ell}^{\operatorname{sph}},\lambda_{2,\ell}^{\operatorname{sph}})\neq 0
for
\ell\not\in S\cup\{\infty\}
by
[
Loe21
, Theorem 2.3]
;

•

For any
\ell\in S
, there exists
\gamma_{\ell}=(\gamma_{0,\ell},\gamma_{1,\ell})\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
such that
\mathfrak{z}_{\ell}(\gamma_{0,\ell}\cdot\varphi_{\ell}^{\operatorname{sph}},\gamma_{1,\ell}\cdot\lambda_{1,\ell}^{\operatorname{sph}},\lambda_{2,\ell}^{\operatorname{sph}})\neq 0
. Indeed, this follows from the
H(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
-invariance of the linear functional.

Putting this all together proves the lemma.
∎

We now discuss the relation with the coherent cohomology pairing
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
. Let
\eta_{\pi,\beta}
,
\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta}
, and
\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta}
be defined in exactly the same way as in §
5.4.1
. For
i=1,2
, let

\omega_{\sigma_{i},\beta}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\widehat{K}^{H_{i}}_{\beta},(-1-d_{i};0)\right)_{\mathbb{C}}

be as in §
5.4.1
. For
\beta\geq 2
, let
\omega_{\sigma_{i},\beta}^{[p]}
denote the cohomology class associated with the
p
-depletion of the good
p
-stabilisation in
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}
(which is a classical modular form of level
\Gamma_{1}(N_{1})\cap\Gamma_{0}(p^{\beta})
for any
\beta\geq 2
). We set
\xi_{2}=0
(so
\zeta_{H_{2}}=(-1-d_{2};0)=(-1-t_{2};0)
), and for
\beta\geq 2
let

\omega^{[p],\operatorname{nearly}}_{\sigma_{1},\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{det}^{r_{2}-1+(t_{1}-d_{1})/2}\nabla^{(t_{1}-d_{1})/2}\omega_{\sigma_{1},\beta}^{[p]}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\widehat{K}^{H_{1}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{1}}\right)_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{nearly}}

where the class
\operatorname{det}
is defined in Definition
6.1.1
. Here
\nabla
denotes the action of the Gauss–Manin connection on nearly holomorphic forms as in
[
GPR23
]
. Note that
\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\omega_{\sigma_{1},\beta}^{[p],\operatorname{nearly}}
is also a cohomology class of level
\widehat{K}^{H_{i}}_{\beta}
(by the definition of tame test data in §
5.3.2
).

Proposition 5.4.6
.

With notation as above, let
\beta\geq 2
. Then there exists a scalar
\Omega_{\pi}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, independent of
\beta
,
\gamma_{S}
, and
\sigma_{1}\boxtimes\sigma_{2}
, such that

\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\omega^{[p],\operatorname{nearly}}_{\sigma_{1},\beta},\omega^{[p]}_{\sigma_{2},\beta})=\mathcal{E}_{p}(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},1/2)\frac{\mathscr{P}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\delta^{(t_{1}-d_{2})/2}\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{sph}},\lambda_{2}^{\operatorname{sph}})}{\Omega_{\pi}}

where
\mathcal{E}_{p}(\cdots)
is the
p
-adic multiplier:

\mathcal{E}_{p}(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},s)=\prod_{i,j,k=1}^{2}\left(1-\frac{p^{-s}}{\theta_{i}\gamma_{j}^{(1)}\gamma_{k}^{(2)}}\right)

where
\{\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{4}\}
are the Satake parameters of
\Pi_{p}
ordered so that
\Theta_{\Pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}})=p^{2}\theta_{1}\theta_{2}
, and
\{\gamma_{1}^{(a)},\gamma_{2}^{(a)}\}
denote the Satake parameters of
\Sigma_{a,p}
for
a=1,2
.

Proof.

Write
\doteq
to mean equal up to a non-zero element of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
independent of
\beta
,
\gamma_{S}
and
\sigma_{1}\boxtimes\sigma_{2}
. Then, by Proposition
3.3.5
(and
[
LPSZ21
, Lemma 6.5]
), we see that

\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{alg}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi,\beta},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\omega^{[p],\operatorname{nearly}}_{\sigma_{1},\beta},\omega^{[p]}_{\sigma_{2},\beta})\doteq p^{4\beta}\mathscr{P}(\gamma_{0,S}\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\delta^{(t_{1}-d_{1})/2}\lambda_{1}^{[p]},\lambda_{2}^{[p]})

where
\lambda_{i}^{[p]}
denotes the
p
-depletion of
\lambda_{i}^{\operatorname{sph}}
(or equivalently the
p
-depletion of the Iwahori level automorphic form in
\Sigma_{i}
corresponding to the good
p
-stabilisation in
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}
), and
\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta}=\phi_{\beta}^{\prime}\otimes|\!|s(-)|\!|^{(r_{1}+r_{2})/2}\in\Pi
with
\phi_{\beta}^{\prime}
as in Proposition
5.4.3
. Note that, by
[
Loe21
, Theorem 2.3]
, there exists a unique
\mathfrak{z}_{p}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(\Pi_{p}\boxtimes\Sigma_{1,p}\boxtimes\Sigma_{2,p},\mathbb{C})
which satisfies
\mathfrak{z}_{p}(\varphi^{\operatorname{sph}}_{p},\lambda_{1,p}^{\operatorname{sph}},\lambda_{2,p})=1
. It therefore suffices to show that:

\mathfrak{z}_{p}(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta,p}^{\prime},\lambda_{1,p}^{[p]},\lambda_{2,p}^{[p]})\doteq p^{-4\beta}\mathcal{E}_{p}(\Pi\times\Sigma_{1}\times\Sigma_{2},1/2).

But this follows from the same strategy as in Appendix
A
using the fact that
\mathfrak{z}_{p}(\gamma\cdot\varphi_{\beta,p}^{\prime},\lambda_{1,p}^{[p]},\lambda_{2,p}^{[p]})=0
(
[
Loe21
, Proposition 5.8]
and the fact that the Whittaker function associated with
\varphi_{\beta,p}^{\prime}
vanishes at the identity for
\beta\geq 2
), and the invariance of
\lambda_{2,p}^{[p]}
under
B_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. The Klingen level statement is simply
[
Loe21
, Proposition 5.8]
(note the differences in the normalisations of the Satake parameters and the trace compatible system of eigenvectors).
∎

6.
Families of automorphic forms and
p
-adic
L
-functions

In this section, we construct the
p
-adic
L
-functions which appear in Theorem
A
and Theorem
C
.

6.1.
Families of Eisenstein series

We briefly recall families of Eisenstein series following
[
LPSZ21
, §7]
and
[
LZ21a
, §10]
. As we are keeping track of the action of the centre for automorphic vector bundles of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
, we introduce the following class:

Definition 6.1.1
.

Set
K=K^{p}K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Let
