M_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}P_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}\times^{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
denote the pushout along the projection to the torus: for any
S\to X_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K)
,
M_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}(S)
is the set of all trivialisations
\psi_{1}\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Lie}(E)
,
\psi_{2}\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\to\omega_{E^{D}}
, where
E
denotes the generalised elliptic curve corresponding to
S\to X_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K)
. We let

\operatorname{det}\colon M_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}

denote the global section sending
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
to
\Psi(1)\in\mathcal{O}_{S}(S)
, where
\Psi\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{S}
is the isomorphism given by the composition:

\mathcal{O}_{S}\xrightarrow{\psi_{1}\otimes\psi_{2}}\operatorname{Lie}(E)\otimes\omega_{E^{D}}\xrightarrow{\lambda\otimes 1}\operatorname{Lie}(E^{D})\otimes\omega_{E^{D}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{S}

where
\lambda\colon\operatorname{Lie}(E)\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Lie}(E^{D})
is induced from the polarisation, and the last map is the natural one. Then
\operatorname{det}\in\operatorname{Fil}_{0}\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\beta,(0;-1)}^{\operatorname{nhol}}
is a holomorphic modular form of weight
(0;-1)
and level
K
, which is invertible as a section of
M_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}
. The restriction of
\operatorname{det}
along the natural map
\mathcal{IG}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{p}U(J_{p}))^{\operatorname{tor}}\to M_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}
is valued in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, hence for any locally analytic character
\chi\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to A^{\times}
, we can define
\operatorname{det}^{\chi}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\chi\circ\operatorname{det}
, which is a
p
-adic modular form of weight
(0;-\chi)
. One can easily show that
\operatorname{det}^{\chi}
is overconvergent, i.e.,
\operatorname{det}^{\chi}\in\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\beta,(0;-\chi)}
.

We now define the families of Eisenstein series.

Definition 6.1.2
.

Let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension and
(A,A^{+})
a complete Tate affinoid pair over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
. Let
\Phi^{(p)}
be a Schwartz function on
(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})^{2}
with values in a number field contained in
L
(via our fixed isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
), and let
\chi^{(p)}
be a Dirichlet character of prime-to-
p
conductor such that one has

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&0\\
0&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot\Phi^{(p)}=\widehat{\chi}^{(p)}(a)^{-1}\Phi^{(p)}

for all
a\in(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{(p)})^{\times}
. Let
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\xi\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to A^{\times}
be locally analytic characters. We let

\mathcal{E}_{\xi}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=\mathcal{E}_{\xi}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2};\chi^{(p)})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{det}^{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}-\xi}\cdot\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2};\chi^{(p)})

where
\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2};\chi^{(p)})
denotes the
p
-adic family of Eisenstein series as in
[
LPSZ21
, Theorem 7.6]
.

Note that by
[
GPR23
, Lemma 2.4.1]
,
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\beta}
is independent of
\beta
, so we will henceforth drop it from the notation.

Lemma 6.1.3
.

Let
\zeta=(-\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1;\xi)
and suppose that
K^{p}
fixes
\Phi^{(p)}
. Then one has
\mathcal{E}_{\xi}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta}
, i.e., the family of Eisenstein series is a weight
\zeta
nearly overconvergent modular form over
A
.

Proof.

The main result of
[
GPR23
]
is that
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}\hat{\otimes}A
comes equipped with an
A
-algebra action
\star
of locally analytic functions
\operatorname{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},A)
such that: the structural map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to A
acts through the Maass–Shimura differential operator
\delta
(which on
q
-expansions is given by
q\frac{d}{dq}
), and the indicator function
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts as
p
-depletion. Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to A
be the locally analytic function which is supported on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and satisfies
\phi(x)=\kappa_{1}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

It is already known (
[
LZ21a
, Proposition 10.1.2]
) that
\mathcal{E}_{\xi-\kappa_{1}}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(0,\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{1})
is overconvergent of weight
(-1-\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{1};\xi-\kappa_{1})
, and one can easily check that the weight
(-\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1;\xi)
nearly overconvergent form
\phi\star\mathcal{E}_{\xi-\kappa_{1}}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(0,\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{1})
has the same
q
-expansion as
\mathcal{E}_{\xi}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})
. This proves the claim.
∎

6.1.1.
Classical specialisations

Let
A=L
and let
\rho
be a Dirichlet character of conductor
p^{\beta^{\prime}}
for some
\beta^{\prime}\geq 0
. Suppose that
\kappa_{1}=a-\rho
,
\kappa_{2}=b+\rho
,
\xi=c
are locally algebraic characters of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, with
a,b\geq 0
and
c
integers. Let
\beta\geq\operatorname{max}(2\operatorname{max}(1,\beta^{\prime}),\operatorname{max}(1,\beta^{\prime})+1)
and let
K^{p}\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be a neat compact open subgroup which fixes
\Phi^{(p)}
. Then one has

\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\operatorname{Fil}_{a}\mathscr{N}^{\operatorname{nhol}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\beta,\zeta},\quad\quad\zeta=(-a-b-1;c),

where
\mathscr{N}^{\operatorname{nhol}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\beta}=\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{p}K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}))_{L},\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P^{\operatorname{an}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}}\right)
is the space of nearly holomorphic forms of level
K\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K^{p}K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Indeed, this follows from
[
LPSZ21
, Proposition 7.3]
and the fact that
\Phi_{p,\rho^{-1},\rho}
(Definition 7.5 in
op.cit.
) is an eigenvector under the action of
K_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(p^{\beta})
with eigencharacter
\widehat{\rho}(\operatorname{det}(-))
.

Moreover, via the identification
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, rigid GAGA, and the description of the coherent cohomology of modular curves in §
3.3
, one can view

(6.1.4)

\operatorname{Fil}_{a}\mathscr{N}^{\operatorname{nhol}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\beta,\zeta}\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}},K^{+}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty})}\left(\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}},\mathscr{A}(\operatorname{GL}_{2})^{K}\right).

Let
v_{\zeta}^{*}\in\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}
denote the element in Notation
2.2.9
. Then, if
\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})
corresponds to the morphism
\mathcal{E}\colon\mathbb{D}_{\overline{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta^{*})^{\operatorname{nearly}}\to\mathscr{A}(\operatorname{GL}_{2})^{K}
via the inclusion in (
6.1.4
), the automorphic form
\mathcal{E}(v_{\zeta}^{*})
is given by

\mathcal{E}(v_{\zeta}^{*})(g)=|\!|\operatorname{det}g|\!|^{\tfrac{1+a+b}{2}-c}\widehat{\rho}(\operatorname{det}g)^{-1}E^{\Phi}(g;\widehat{\chi},\tfrac{b-a+1}{2}),\quad\quad g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}),

where
\chi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\chi^{(p)}\rho^{-2}
and
E^{\Phi}(-;\widehat{\chi},s)
is the Eisenstein series in
[
LPSZ21
, §9.2]
with respect to the Schwartz function
\Phi=\Phi^{(a+b+1)}_{\infty}\Phi^{(p)}\Phi_{p,\rho^{-1},\rho}
(with notation as in
[
LPSZ21
, Definition 7.5, §8.5.1]
).

Remark 6.1.5
.

As explained in
[
LPSZ21
, Theorem 7.6]
, the family of Eisenstein series
\mathcal{E}_{\xi}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})
is zero on the component of
\operatorname{Spec}A
where
\kappa_{1}(-1)\kappa_{2}(-1)\neq-\chi^{(p)}(-1)
.

6.2.
Coleman families

In this section, we introduce the Coleman families for
G
and
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
that will provide the input into the
p
-adic
L
-functions. Our definition differs slightly from the literature since we do not want to fix a “base-point” for the family, however we explain in Examples
6.2.3
and
6.2.9
that any good
p
-stabilisation has a Coleman family passing through it (in our sense).

6.2.1.
Coleman families for
\operatorname{GL}_{2}

Let
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
denote the adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
whose
S
-points parameterise continuous characters
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(S)^{\times}
. Let
N\geq 1
be an integer prime to
p
, and
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
a finite extension containing
\mu_{N}
. Let
\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}=\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(N)
denote the Coleman–Mazur–Buzzard cuspidal eigencurve over
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
of tame level
\Gamma_{1}(N)
. We normalise the weight map
w\colon\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}\to\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
such that the weight of a point
x\in\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
is given by
w(x)+1
.

We consider the following special locus of points in this eigencurve.

Notation 6.2.1
.

Let
\chi
be a Dirichlet character of conductor dividing
N
. We let
\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}\subset\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
denote the subset of points corresponding to good
p
-stabilised automorphic representations
\widetilde{\sigma}
of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
of weight
1+t
, level
N
, and character
\chi
, for some
t\geq 0
. We note that the weight map
w
is étale at any point in
\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}
(see
[
Bel12
,
BD16
]
).

We now introduce the definition of Coleman families for
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
.

Definition 6.2.2
.

Let
U=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{U},\mathscr{O}_{U}^{+})
be a reduced affinoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
. A Coleman family
\underline{\sigma}
over
U
, of tame level
N
and character
\chi
, is a morphism
f\colon U\to\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
such that
\Upsilon(\underline{\sigma})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f^{-1}(\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}^{\operatorname{good},\chi})
is Zariski dense in
U
.

Example 6.2.3
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}
is a good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
of weight
1+t
, level
N
, and character
\chi
, for some
t\geq 0
. After possibly enlarging
L
, let
x_{t}\in\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(L)
denote the corresponding point on the eigencurve. Then there exists a sufficiently small open neighbourhood
U\subset\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
of
w(x_{t})=t
and a Coleman family
\underline{\sigma}
over
U
(of tame level
N
and character
\chi
) passing through
x_{t}
, i.e., one has
f(t)=x_{t}
, where
f\colon U\to\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
denotes the Coleman family. Indeed, this follows from the fact that
w
is étale at the point
x_{t}
.

To any Coleman family
\underline{\sigma}
as above, one can associate a family of cohomology classes. More precisely, let
T
be a set of rational primes which do not divide
Np
, and let
K_{T}\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{T})
be a normal compact open subgroup. For
\ell^{n_{\ell}}\mid\mid N
with
n_{\ell}\geq 1
, we let
K_{1}(\ell^{n_{\ell}})\subset\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
denote the subgroup of matrices
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)
satisfying
c\equiv 0
and
d\equiv 1
modulo
\ell^{n_{\ell}}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
. We set

K^{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K_{T}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\nmid Np\\
v\notin T\end{subarray}}\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{v})\cdot\prod_{\ell^{n_{\ell}}\mid\mid N}K_{1}(\ell^{n_{\ell}})\quad\subset\quad\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})

and assume that
K_{T}
is chosen in such a way that
K^{p}
is a neat compact open subgroup. Set
K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\beta}=K^{p}K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Then, associated with
f\colon U\to\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
, there exists a cohomology class

\omega_{\underline{\sigma}}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\beta},\zeta_{U}\right)^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},+),\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}\quad\quad(n\gg 0)

which is an eigenvector for the action of (normalised) Hecke operators at
p
, the Hecke operators
U_{\ell}
for
\ell|N
, and the Hecke operators
T_{\ell}
for
\ell\nmid Np
with eigencharacter determined by the morphism
f
. Here
\zeta_{U}=(-1-\kappa_{U};0)
where
\kappa_{U}
denotes the pullback of the universal character under the composition
w\circ f\colon U\to\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
. This cohomology class satisfies the following specialisation property: for any
x\in\Upsilon(\underline{\sigma})
, the specialisation of
\omega_{\underline{\sigma}}
at
x
is the restriction of a classical cohomology class

\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{x}}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\beta},\zeta_{x}\right)^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}=\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\beta}),[V_{T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{x})]\right)^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}},\quad\zeta_{x}=(-1-w(x);0),

which we can view as a morphism

\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{x}}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}},K^{+}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\infty})}\left(V_{T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}(\zeta_{x})^{*},\mathscr{A}(\operatorname{GL}_{2})^{K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\beta}}\right)

via rigid GAGA, the identification in Proposition
3.3.1
, and the identification
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Moreover, the automorphic form
\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{x}}(1)
is equal to the good
p
-stabilisation in the good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation
(\underline{\sigma}_{x},\alpha_{x})
corresponding to the point
x
.

Remark 6.2.4
.

In the definition of a Coleman family, one often assumes that
f\colon U\to\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
is an open immersion; for maximal flexibility, we allow more general morphisms. In particular, we allow the degenerate case of
f\colon\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\to\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
corresponding to a point in
\mathscr{E}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}
.

6.2.2.
Coleman families for
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}

Let
\mathcal{W}((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{\oplus 2})
denote the adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
whose
S
-points parameterise continuous characters
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{\oplus 2}\to\mathcal{O}(S)^{\times}
. Let
(N,M)
be a pair of positive integers prime to
p
such that
M^{2}|N
, and let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension. Let
T
be a finite set of primes not dividing
Np
, and let
K_{T}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})
be a normal compact open subgroup. We set

K^{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K_{T}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\nmid Np\\
\ell\not\in T\end{subarray}}G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\cdot\prod_{\ell|N}K(N,M)_{\ell}\quad\subset\quad G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})

where
K(N,M)_{\ell}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
denotes the
\ell
-component of the quasi-paramodular subgroup of level
(N,M)
. We assume that
K^{p}
is neat. Let
S
denote the set of primes dividing
N
.

We let
\mathscr{E}_{G}=\mathscr{E}_{G}(N,M)
denote the cuspidal eigenvariety
10
10

10

Obtained from considering only the “
-
” versions of the cohomology groups of overconvergent forms.
constructed in
[
BP21
, §6.9]
of tame quasi-paramodular level
K(N,M)
– in particular, there is a weight map
w\colon\mathscr{E}_{G}\to\mathcal{W}((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{\oplus 2})
and morphisms
f\colon U\to\mathscr{E}_{G}
out of affinoid adic spaces
U=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{U},\mathscr{O}_{U}^{+})
correspond to finite-slope Hecke eigensystems
(\lambda^{S},\lambda_{p})\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathscr{O}_{U}}^{S}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{U}[T^{G,-}]\to\mathscr{O}_{U}
appearing in the cohomology

(6.2.5)

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{{}^{M}W_{G}}\\
k\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{H}^{k}_{w,\operatorname{an}}\left(K^{p},\nu_{U},\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{-,\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}

for some
h\in\mathbb{Q}
for which there exist slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to
s_{p,\operatorname{B}}\in T^{G,--}
(with notation as in
loc.cit.
). Here
\nu_{U}=(r_{1,U},r_{2,U};-(r_{1,U}+r_{2,U}))
where
(r_{1,U},r_{2,U})
denotes the pullback of the universal character under
w\circ f\colon U\to\mathcal{W}((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{\oplus 2})
. We note that

\operatorname{H}^{i}_{w_{1},\operatorname{an}}\left(K^{p},\nu_{U},\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{-,\leq h}\cong\operatorname{H}^{i}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{U}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-),\leq h},\quad\quad(i\in\mathbb{Z}),

for any
\beta\geq 1
and sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, where
\kappa_{U}=(r_{2,U}+2,-r_{1,U};-r_{2,U}-1)
.

Notation 6.2.6
.

Let
\chi
be a Dirichlet character of conductor
M
. We let
\mathscr{E}_{G}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}\subset\mathscr{E}_{G}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
denote the subset of points corresponding to small slope good
p
-stabilised automorphic representations
\widetilde{\pi}=(\pi,\Theta_{\pi,p})
of
G(\mathbb{A})
of weight
\nu=(r_{1},r_{2};-(r_{1}+r_{2}))
, level
(N,M)
and character
\chi
, for some
r_{1}\geq r_{2}\geq 0
. We note that the Hecke eigensystem corresponding such a point in
\mathscr{E}_{G}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}
is given by
(\lambda^{S},\lambda_{p})
satisfying
\lambda^{S}=\Theta^{S}_{\pi}
and
\lambda_{p}|_{T^{G,-}}=w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\Theta_{\pi,p}^{+}
. Let
I\subset\mathcal{H}^{S}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{p}[T^{G,-}]
denote the kernel of
\lambda^{S}\otimes\lambda_{p}
.. By the classicality and vanishing results of higher Coleman theory (
[
BP21
, Corollary 6.8.4, Theorem 5.12.3]
supplemented with
[
LZ21a
, Proposition 2.7.2]
and
[
BP
, Theorem 1.4.10]
), we see that

(6.2.7)

\operatorname{H}^{i}_{w,\operatorname{an}}\left(K^{p},\nu,\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{-,\operatorname{fs},G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I}=0\quad\text{ if }\quad i\neq 3-l(w).

Furthermore, if
\pi
is non-endoscopic and
i=3-l(w)
, then the localised cohomology group in (
6.2.7
) is one-dimensional over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
for all
w\in{{}^{M}W_{G}}
. If
\pi
is of Yoshida-type and
i=3-l(w)
, then the localised cohomology group in (
6.2.7
) is one-dimensional over
\mathbb{C}_{p}
for
l(w)=1,2
, and vanishes when
l(w)=0,3
. In both cases, a Tor-spectral sequence argument shows that the weight map
w
is étale at points in
\mathscr{E}_{G}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}
(see
[
LZ21a
, §3.4]
).

We now introduce Coleman families for
G
.

Definition 6.2.8
.

Let
U=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{U},\mathscr{O}_{U}^{+})
be a reduced finite-type affinoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
. Let
|\!|\cdot|\!|
denote the Banach norm on
\mathscr{O}_{U}
satisfying
|\!|p|\!|=p^{-1}
. A Coleman family
\underline{\pi}
over
U
, of tame level
(N,M)
and character
\chi
, is a morphism
f\colon U\to\mathscr{E}_{G}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
such that:

(1)

\Upsilon(\underline{\pi})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f^{-1}(\mathscr{E}_{G}^{\operatorname{good},\chi})
is Zariski dense in
U
.

(2)

The cohomology groups
\operatorname{H}^{k}_{w,\operatorname{an}}\left(K^{p},\nu_{U},\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{-}
admit slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to the action of
s_{p,\operatorname{B}}
for some
h
satisfying
|\!|\lambda_{p}(s_{p,\operatorname{B}})|\!|\geq p^{-h}
. If
I_{U}
denotes the kernel of
\lambda^{S}\otimes\lambda_{p}
, then

\operatorname{H}^{k}_{w,\operatorname{an}}\left(K^{p},\nu_{U},\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{-,\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}=0

if
k\neq 3-l(w)
, and either:
S^{k}(\underline{\pi})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{H}^{k}_{w_{3-k},\operatorname{an}}\left(K^{p},\nu_{U},\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{-,\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}
is free of rank one over
\mathscr{O}_{U}
for all
k=0,1,2,3
(general-type); or
S^{0}(\underline{\pi})=S^{3}(\underline{\pi})=0
and
S^{k}(\underline{\pi})
is free of rank one over
\mathscr{O}_{U}
for
k=1,2
(Yoshida-type).

(3)

For
K^{G}_{\beta}=K^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
with
K^{p}
as above,
\kappa_{G}=(r_{2,U}+2,-r_{1,U};-r_{2,U}-1)
, and
n\geq 1
sufficiently large, the cohomology groups in (
4.4.12
) admit slope
\leq h
decompositions with respect to
s_{p,\operatorname{B}}
and the map is surjective on slope
\leq h
parts.

Example 6.2.9
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\pi}
is a good small slope
p
-stabilised automorphic representation of
G(\mathbb{A})
of weight
\nu=(r_{1},r_{2};-(r_{1}+r_{2}))
, tame level
(N,M)
and character
\chi
. After possibly enlarging
L
, let
x\in\mathscr{E}_{G}^{\operatorname{good},\chi}
denote the corresponding point of the eigenvariety. Then since the weight map is étale at this point, there exists a Coleman family
\underline{\pi}
over a sufficiently small open neighbourhood
U
of
w(x)
in
\mathcal{W}((\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})^{\oplus 2})
passing through
\widetilde{\pi}
(one can always shrink
U
so that properties (2) and (3) of Definition
6.2.8
are satisfied). The degenerate case of the point
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}})\to\mathscr{E}_{G}
corresponding to
x
is also included in our definition of a Coleman family.

To any Coleman family
\underline{\pi}
as above, we can choose
\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
to be a
\mathscr{O}_{U}
-basis of

\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-),\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}\cong S^{2}(\underline{\pi})

which is independent of the choice of
n
, and
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}(\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta+1})=\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
(where
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}
denotes the unnormalised trace map). Furthermore, if
x\in\Upsilon(\underline{\pi})
and
(\underline{\pi}_{x},\Theta_{\underline{\pi}_{x},p})
denotes the corresponding small slope good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation, then the specialisation of
\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
at
x
is (up to a non-zero scalar multiple) equal to the class
\eta_{\underline{\pi}_{x},\beta}
from §
5.4.1
via the classicality results of higher Coleman theory.

6.3.
Interpolation regions

We now introduce the interpolation regions for the
p
-adic
L
-functions.

6.3.1.

Suppose we are in the setting of Case A (§
5.3.1
). Let
\underline{\pi}
be a Coleman family for
G
over
U_{0}
of tame level
(N_{0},M_{0})
and character
\chi_{0}
. Let
\underline{\sigma}
be a Coleman family for
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
over
U_{2}
of tame level
N_{2}
and character
\chi_{2}
. We let

\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}=\left\{(x,j+\chi,y)\in\Upsilon(\underline{\pi})\times\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})\times\Upsilon(\underline{\sigma}):\begin{array}[]{c}0\leq t_{2}\leq r_{1}-r_{2},\;0\leq j\leq r_{1}-r_{2}-t_{2}\\
w(x)=(r_{1},r_{2}),\;w(y)=t_{2}\\
\chi\text{ finite-order }\end{array}\right\}.

This region is precisely the range of specialisations where the
L
-function has critical values, i.e., if
\underline{\pi}_{x}
and
\underline{\sigma}_{y}
denote the specialisations of the Coleman families and
\underline{\Pi}_{x}
and
\underline{\Sigma}_{y}
their unitary normalisations, then
L(\underline{\Pi}_{x}\times\underline{\Sigma}_{y},\chi^{-1},j+\tfrac{1-r_{1}+r_{2}+t_{2}}{2})
is a critical
L
-value for
(x,j+\chi,y)\in\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}
. Note that if
\underline{\pi}
and
\underline{\sigma}
are Coleman families as in Examples
6.2.8
and
6.2.3
respectively (over open subsets of weight space), then this critical interpolation region
\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}
is Zariski dense.

Remark 6.3.1
.

The inequalities in this definition describe “region (f)” in
[
LZ21b
, Figure 2]
.

6.3.2.

We also consider the interpolation region (f) in Case B (§
5.3.2
). More precisely, let
\underline{\pi}
be a Coleman family for
G
over
U_{0}
of tame level
(N_{0},M_{0})
and character
\chi_{0}
. For
i=1,2
, let
\underline{\sigma}_{i}
be Coleman families for
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
over
U_{i}
of tame level
N_{i}
and character
\chi_{i}
. We consider the region:

\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}=\left\{(x,y_{1},y_{2})\in\Upsilon(\underline{\pi})\times\Upsilon(\underline{\sigma}_{1})\times\Upsilon(\underline{\sigma}_{2}):\begin{array}[]{c}0\leq d_{2}\leq r_{1}-r_{2},\;0\leq d_{1}\leq r_{1}-r_{2}-d_{2}\\
w(x)=(r_{1},r_{2}),\;w(y_{1})=d_{1},\;w(y_{2})=d_{2}\end{array}\right\}.

In this region, we will consider the automorphic period

\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{sph}}(x,y_{1},y_{2};\gamma_{S})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{P}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\underline{\varphi}^{\operatorname{sph}}_{x},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\delta^{(r_{1}-r_{2}-d_{1}-d_{2})/2}\underline{\lambda}_{1,y_{1}}^{\operatorname{sph}},\underline{\lambda}_{2,y_{2}}^{\operatorname{sph}}),\quad\quad(x,y_{1},y_{2})\in\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}

which is a substitute for the central critical
L
-value
L(\underline{\Pi}_{x}\times\underline{\Sigma}_{1,y_{1}}\times\underline{\Sigma}_{2,y_{2}},1/2)
. Here the notation is as in §
5.4.2
(i.e.,
\underline{\varphi}_{x}^{\operatorname{sph}}
is the new vector in
\underline{\Pi}_{x}
etc.).

6.4.
p
-adic
L
-functions for
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}

We now construct the
p
-adic
L
-function in Coleman families. We place ourselves in Case A (§
5.3.1
) and let
\underline{\pi}
and
\underline{\sigma}
be Coleman families for
G
and
\operatorname{GL}_{2}
respectively, as in §
6.3.1
.

Theorem 6.4.1
.

There exists a locally analytic distribution
\mathscr{L}_{p}\in\mathcal{O}(U_{0}\times\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})\times U_{2})
such that: for all
(x,j+\chi,y)\in\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}
and
\beta\geq\operatorname{max}(2\operatorname{max}(1,c(\chi)),\operatorname{max}(1,c(\chi))+1)
, there exists a non-zero scalar
c_{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
(independent of
j
,
\chi
,
\beta
, and
\gamma_{S}
) such that

\mathscr{L}_{p}(x,j+\chi,y)=c_{x}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{alg}}(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi}_{x},\beta},\omega_{\operatorname{Eis},j,\beta},\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{y},\beta})

where the inputs in the right-hand side are as in §
5.4.1
, and we compare both sides via the identification
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Proof.

Let
\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
and
\omega_{\underline{\sigma}}
be the cohomology classes from §
6.2.2
and §
6.2.1
. By property (3) in Definition
6.2.8
and the fact that
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}
is an isomorphism of finite-slope parts, we can lift
\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
to classes

\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta}\in\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-),\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}

which satisfy
\operatorname{Tr}_{G,\beta}(\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta+1})=\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
. Let
x\in\Upsilon{\underline{\pi}}
. We have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-),\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{x}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,-),\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{x}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\operatorname{nearly},\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},-),\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{x}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\dagger,-),\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{x}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{\leq h,G(\mathbb{Z}_{T})/K_{T}}_{I_{U}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sp}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{sp}_{x}}

where
\operatorname{sp}_{x}
denotes specialisation at
x
and projecting to the overconvergent cohomology of the dual of the (nearly) algebraic representation of highest weight
\kappa_{x}=(r_{2}+2,-r_{1};-r_{2}-1)
with
w(x)=(r_{1},r_{2})
, the left and centre vertical maps are the natural ones, the right-hand horizontal maps denote the classicality morphisms from higher Coleman theory and Proposition
4.4.8
, and
f=(f_{1}^{\vee})^{-1}\circ f_{2}^{\vee}
with notation as in §
5.4.1
. This implies that the image of
\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
in the right-hand top corner of the above diagram is (up to a scalar
c_{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
independent of
\beta
) equal to the unique lift of
\eta_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
under
f
.

Let
U_{1}^{(0)}\subset U_{1}^{(1)}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
be an increasing cover by quasi-compact open affinoid subspaces
U_{1}^{(i)}=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{U_{1}^{(i)}},\mathscr{O}_{U_{1}^{(i)}}^{+})
such that the universal character
\kappa_{i}
is
i
-analytic. Set
\lambda=r_{2,U_{0}}+t_{U_{2}}+1
and
\xi=1-r_{2,U_{0}}
, where
t_{U_{2}}
denotes the universal character over
U_{2}
. Let
n\gg i
. We define

\Xi_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}_{\lambda,\beta}\left(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta},\mathcal{E}_{\xi}^{\Phi^{(p)}}(r_{1,U_{0}}-r_{2,U_{0}}-t_{U_{2}}-\kappa_{i},\kappa_{i}),\omega_{\underline{\sigma}}\right)\quad\in\quad\mathscr{O}_{U_{0}}\hat{\otimes}\mathscr{O}_{U^{(i)}_{1}}\hat{\otimes}\mathscr{O}_{U_{2}}

which is independent of
n
and
\beta
(see Lemma
4.5.12
). Here
\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
denotes the image of the class
\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi},\beta}
under the pullback map
\operatorname{H}^{2}(K^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}\to\operatorname{H}^{2}(\widehat{K}^{G}_{\beta},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp})^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}
. Furthermore, these classes are compatible with changing
i
, so we can define

\mathscr{L}_{p}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\Xi_{i})_{i\geq 0}\in\varprojlim_{i\geq 0}\mathscr{O}_{U_{0}}\hat{\otimes}\mathscr{O}_{U^{(i)}_{1}}\hat{\otimes}\mathscr{O}_{U_{2}}=\mathcal{O}(U_{0}\times\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})\times U_{2}).

The interpolation property now follows from Lemma
4.5.10
and Lemma
4.5.5
.
∎

Combining this theorem with Proposition
5.4.3
completes the proof of Theorem
A
in the introduction.

6.5.
p
-adic
L
-functions for
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}

Suppose we are in the setting of Case B (§
5.3.2
) and let
\underline{\pi}
and
\underline{\sigma}_{i}
be as in §
6.3.2
. Recall that we are assuming that
p>2
.

Theorem 6.5.1
.

There exists a rigid-analytic function
\mathscr{L}_{p}\in\mathcal{O}(U_{0}\times U_{1}\times U_{2})
such that: for all
(x,y_{1},y_{2})\in\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}
and
\beta\geq 2
, there exists a non-zero scalar
c_{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
(independent of
\beta
,
\gamma_{S}
,
\underline{\sigma}_{1}
, and
\underline{\sigma}_{2}
) such that

\mathscr{L}_{p}(x,y_{1},y_{2})=c_{x}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\operatorname{alg}}\left(\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\underline{\pi}_{x},\beta},\gamma_{1,S}\cdot\omega^{[p],\operatorname{nearly}}_{\underline{\sigma}_{1,y_{1}},\beta},\omega^{[p]}_{\underline{\sigma}_{2,y_{2}},\beta}\right)

where the inputs in the right-hand side are as in §
5.4.2
, and we compare both sides via the identification
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

Proof.

This follows from exactly the same strategy as in Theorem
6.4.1
replacing the family of Eisenstein series with the family of nearly overconvergent modular forms

\omega^{[p],\operatorname{nearly}}_{\underline{\sigma}_{1},\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{det}^{(r_{1,U_{0}}+r_{2,U_{0}}-d_{U_{1}}-d_{U_{2}}-2)/2}\nabla^{(r_{1,U_{0}}-r_{2,U_{0}}-d_{U_{1}}-d_{U_{2}})/2}\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{1},\beta}^{[p]}\;\in\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}\hat{\otimes}\mathscr{O}_{U_{0}\times U_{1}\times U_{2}}

and replacing
\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{2},\beta}
with its
p
-depleted version
\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{2},\beta}^{[p]}
. Here
d_{U_{i}}
is the universal character of
U_{i}
(determined by its map to one-dimensional weight space). More precisely, we have

\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{2},\beta}^{[p]}=1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\star\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{2},\beta},\quad\nabla^{(r_{1,U_{0}}-r_{2,U_{0}}-d_{U_{1}}-d_{U_{2}})/2}\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{1},\beta}^{[p]}=(\rho 1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}})\star\omega_{\underline{\sigma}_{1},\beta}

where
\rho\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathscr{O}_{U_{0}\times U_{1}\times U_{2}}^{\times}
is a square-root of
r_{1,U_{0}}-r_{2,U_{0}}-d_{U_{1}}-d_{U_{2}}
(which exists because, by assumption, there exists a Zariski dense subset of points where this quantity specialises to an even integer),
\star
denotes the action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathscr{O}_{U_{0}\times U_{1}\times U_{2}})
on nearly overconvergent modular forms in
[
GPR23
]
, and
1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathscr{O}_{U_{0}\times U_{1}\times U_{2}})
denotes the indicator function of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. By abuse of notation,
\rho 1_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}
denotes the locally analytic function on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
obtained by extending
\rho
by zero.
∎

Combining this theorem with Proposition
5.4.6
, we therefore obtain a locally analytic distribution interpolating
\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{sph}}(x,y_{1},y_{2};\gamma_{S})
for
(x,y_{1},y_{2})\in\Upsilon^{\operatorname{crit}}
, completing the proof of Theorem
C
in the introduction.

7.
Temperedness of the distribution

In this section we prove Theorem
B
; namely, if we assume we’re in Case A (§
5.3.1
) and fix a small slope good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation
\widetilde{\pi}=(\pi,\Theta_{\pi,p})
(resp. good
p
-stabilised automorphic representation
\widetilde{\sigma}
) of
G(\mathbb{A})
(resp.
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
) of weight
\nu=(r_{1},r_{2};-(r_{1}+r_{2}))
, level
(N_{0},M_{0})
and character
\chi_{0}
(resp. of weight
1+t_{2}
, level
N_{2}
, and character
\chi_{2}
) such that
t_{2}\leq r_{1}-r_{2}
, then we prove the following theorem:

Theorem 7.0.1
.

Let
L_{p}(\pi\times\sigma,-)\in\mathscr{D}^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},L)
denote the locally analytic distribution constructed in Theorem
6.4.1
, and let
h=v_{p}\left(\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}^{\circ})\right)
. Then
L_{p}(\pi\times\sigma,-)
is tempered of growth
\leq h+2
in the sense of
[
BDJ21
, Definition 3.10]
. Moreover, if
h+2<r_{1}-r_{2}-t_{2}+1
, then
L_{p}(\pi\times\sigma,-)
is uniquely determined (as a tempered distribution of growth
\leq h+2
) by its values
L_{p}(\pi\times\sigma,j+\chi)
for
0\leq j\leq r_{1}-r_{2}-t_{2}
and
\chi
finite-order.

Remark 7.0.2
.

This is not the optimal growth bound. We expect that
L_{p}(\pi\times\sigma,-)
is tempered of growth
h
, however we were unable to show this.

We note that the last part of this theorem follows from
[
Col98
, Proposition I.4.5]
, so we will focus on proving that the
p
-adic
L
-function is tempered of growth
\leq h+2
for the rest of this section. Concretely this amounts to checking the following property. For
n\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{W}_{n}\subset\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})_{L}
denote the quasi-Stein open subspace of “
(n-1)
-accessible weights” as in
[
LZ16
, Definition 4.1.1]
. By
[
LZ16
, Lemma 4.1.5]
, for any open affinoid
U\subset\mathcal{W}_{n}
, the universal character
\kappa_{U}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(U)^{\times}
is
n
-analytic (i.e., given by a power series on cosets of the form
a+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
). If
U_{n}\subset\mathcal{W}_{n}
is open affinoid, then the sections
\mathcal{O}(U_{n})
naturally form a
L
-Banach algebra with norm
|\!|-|\!|_{n}
such that the unit ball is
\mathcal{O}^{+}(U_{n})
. Let
\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{O}(U_{n})
denote the restriction of
L_{p}(\pi\times\sigma,-)\in\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})_{L})
. Then we need to show that there exists a constant
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
|\!|\mu_{n}|\!|_{n}\leq p^{(h+2)n}C
for all
n\geq 1
and
U_{n}\subset\mathcal{W}_{n}
.

7.0.1.

We first need to understand integrality properties of the family of nearly overconvergent Eisenstein series. Recall that the spaces
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{i}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\widehat{K}^{H_{i}}_{\beta},\zeta_{H_{i}}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},+)}
are independent of
\beta
, so we may take
\beta=1
from now on (and often drop it from the notation). We define an integral version of this space as

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{i}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{V}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(V,[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\circ}]\right)

which is again independent of
\beta
. Here the colimit is over all strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{X}_{H_{i},\operatorname{id},\beta}
, one defines the
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}
-representation
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\circ}
in exactly the same way as for
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})
but replacing
\mathbb{A}^{1,\operatorname{an}}
with
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle t\rangle,\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle t\rangle)
, and the sheaf
[\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\circ}]
is given by

\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{P}_{H_{i},\operatorname{dR},n}}\hat{\otimes}\;\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}}(\zeta_{H_{i}})^{\circ}\right)^{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}^{\square}}.

We will need a strengthening of Lemma
6.1.3
.

Lemma 7.0.3
.

Let
\kappa_{n}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}(U_{n})^{\times}
denote the universal character,
\xi=1-r_{2}
, and
\zeta_{H_{1}}=(-1-t_{1};\xi)
. Let
\zeta_{H_{1},n}
denote
\zeta_{H_{1}}
viewed as a character valued in
\mathcal{O}(U_{n})^{\times}
. Then there exist integers
M_{1},M_{2}\geq 1
(independent of
n
and
U_{n}
) such that

\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi}(t_{1}-\kappa_{n},\kappa_{n})\in p^{-2n-M_{2}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,(n+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{1},n}}.

Proof.

Set
(A,A^{+})=(\mathcal{O}(U_{n}),\mathcal{O}^{+}(U_{n}))
for ease of notation. Set
\lambda_{H_{1},n}=\zeta_{H_{1},n}+(t_{1}-\kappa_{n})(2;-1)
and consider the family of Eisenstein series
\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi+\kappa_{n}-t_{1}}(0,2\kappa_{n}-t_{1})\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}\hat{\otimes}_{L}A
. We claim that there exists an integer
M_{3}\geq 0
(independent of
n
and
U_{n}
) such that

\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi+\kappa_{n}-t_{1}}(0,2\kappa_{n}-t_{1})\in p^{-M_{3}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\lambda_{H_{1},n}}.

Indeed, we note that this family of Eisenstein series is
overconvergent
, hence the analyticity is determined by the analyticity of the weight. Furthermore, integrality (for overconvergent forms) can be checked on the level of
q
-expansions, and it is easy to see that the integrality of the
q
-expansion of this family of Eisenstein series only depends on
\Phi^{(p)}
(see
[
LPSZ21
, Theorem 7.6]
).

Recall from Lemma
6.1.3
that
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},A}
comes equipped with an action
\star
of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},A)
. The image of
\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi}(t_{1}-\kappa_{n},\kappa_{n})
in
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},A}
can be described as

\phi\star\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi+\kappa_{n}-t_{1}}(0,2\kappa_{n}-t_{1})

where
\phi\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to A
is the extension-by-
0
of the character
t_{1}-\kappa_{n}
. It therefore suffices to show that there exist integers
M_{1},M_{2}\geq 1
(independent of
n
and
U_{n}
) such that
\phi\star-
maps
p^{-M_{3}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,(n+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\lambda_{H_{1},n}}
into
p^{-2n-M_{2}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,(n+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{1},n}}
. But this follows from Proposition
B.0.1
by taking
\varepsilon=(p^{n}(p-1))^{-1}
.
∎

7.0.2.

We now study the integrality of the trilinear period in §
4.5.2
. We continue to work at level
\beta=1
and omit
\beta
from the notation. For brevity, we set
\mathcal{Q}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{P}^{\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an}}_{\lambda}
with
\lambda=r_{1}+1-t_{1}
. We let
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\circ}
denote the integral version of
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})
defined in exactly the same way, but replacing
\mathbb{A}^{1,\operatorname{an}}
with
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{+}
(and taking continuous linear functionals valued in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
). We use similar notation for the other
p
-adic nearly representations. Note that the branching law
\operatorname{br}^{n\operatorname{-an}}
preserves integrality. We let
[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\circ}]
denote the sheaf associated with the representation
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\circ}
using
+
-sheaves. We use similar notation for the other
p
-adic nearly representations. We can also twist these sheaves by
\mathcal{O}^{+}(-D_{G})
and
\mathcal{O}^{+}(-D_{H})
.

We define
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{G,\kappa_{G}^{*}}
to be the image of the map

\varinjlim_{V}\operatorname{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,<1}\cap V}\left(V,[\mathbb{D}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,n+1}}(\kappa_{G}^{*})^{\circ}](-D_{G})\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,n\operatorname{-an},-)}

where the colimit is over all strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{1}}
.

Lemma 7.0.4
.

With notation as in Lemma
7.0.3
, there exists an integer
M_{3}\geq 0
(independent
n
and
U_{n}
) such that

\mathcal{Q}_{m}\left(\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{G,\kappa_{G}^{*}},\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{1},n}},\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{2}}}\right)\subset p^{-M_{3}}\mathcal{O}^{+}(U_{n})

for any
m\geq 1
, where
\zeta_{H_{2}}=(-1-t_{2};0)
.

Proof.

Set
\widehat{K}_{H}=(\widehat{K}^{p}\cap H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p)
. Due to the functoriality of the torsors
\mathcal{P}_{?,\operatorname{dR},m}
, one can show (by the same construction for
\mathcal{Q}_{m}
) that for any
x\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{G,\kappa_{G}^{*}}
,
y\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{1},n}}
, and
z\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\zeta_{H_{2}}}
, the element
\hat{\iota}^{*}(x)\smile(y\sqcup z)
is in the image of the map

(7.0.5)

\operatorname{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})_{A},[V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})^{\circ}]\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{2}_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})_{A},[V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})]\right).

Here
A=\mathcal{O}(U_{n})
, we have used excision to write the cohomology groups in this particular way, and
\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}}
denotes the half-sum of the positive roots of
H
not lying in
M_{H}
. Note that
[V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})]\cong\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})}
and
[V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})]^{\circ}\subset\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})}
is an
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})}
-lattice. Hence, up to multiplying by a power of
p
which is independent of
n
and
U_{n}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
[V_{M_{H}}(-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})]^{\circ}\cong\Omega^{2,+}_{\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})}
, where
\Omega^{2,+}_{\mathcal{X}_{H}(\widehat{K}_{H})}
denotes the wedge square of the sheaf of integral differentials. Let
\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id}}
denote the dagger space associated with
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}
. Then the cohomology groups in (
7.0.5
) can be identified with the compactly supported cohomology of
\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id}}
. But
\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id}}
admits a smooth weak formal model
\mathfrak{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id}}
, hence the map in (
7.0.5
) is identified with

\operatorname{H}^{2}_{c}\left(\mathfrak{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id},A^{+}},\Omega^{2}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id},A^{+}}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{2}_{c}\left(\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id},A},\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id},A}}\right).

In particular, there is a trace map
\operatorname{tr}\colon\operatorname{H}^{2}_{c}\left(\mathfrak{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id},A^{+}},\Omega^{2}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\dagger}_{H,\operatorname{id},A^{+}}}\right)\to A^{+}
compatible with the trace map on the generic fibre. This implies the required integrality.
∎

We almost have all the ingredients needed to prove Theorem
7.0.1
. More precisely, the distribution
\mu_{n}
can be described as

\mu_{n}=\mathcal{Q}_{(n+M_{1})}\left(\eta^{(n+M_{1})},\mathcal{E}^{\Phi^{(p)}}_{\xi}(t_{1}-\kappa_{n},\kappa_{n}),\omega_{\sigma}\right)

where
\eta^{(n+M_{1})}
denotes the
(n+M_{1})
-analytic class
\gamma_{0,S}\cdot\widetilde{\eta}_{\pi}
. Note that there exists an integer
M_{4}\geq 0
(independent of
n
and
U_{n}
) such that
p^{M_{4}}\omega_{\sigma}
is integral. Let
\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Theta_{\pi,p}(U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}^{\circ})
. Suppose that we can find an integer
M_{5}\geq 0
(independent of
n
and
U_{n}
) such that
p^{M_{5}}\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}^{m}\eta^{(m)}
is integral for all
m\geq 1
. Then Lemma
7.0.3
and Lemma
7.0.4
imply that

\mu_{n}\in p^{-M_{5}}\alpha^{-(n+M_{1})}_{\operatorname{Kl}}p^{-2n-M_{2}}p^{-M_{4}-M_{3}}\mathcal{O}^{+}(U_{n})

hence we have

|\!|\mu_{n}|\!|_{n}\leq p^{n(h+2)}C

where
C=p^{M_{5}+M_{1}h+M_{2}+M_{4}+M_{3}}
is independent of
n
and
U_{n}
. Here we have used that, by definition,
|\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}|=p^{-h}
. It therefore remains to understand the potential denominators of the classes
\{\eta^{(m)}\}_{m\geq 1}
.

7.0.3.

We keep the notation of the previous two sections. We will study the correspondence associated with
s_{\operatorname{Kl}}=w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}t_{\operatorname{Kl}}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}
.

Lemma 7.0.6
.

For any
m\geq 1
, one has a factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,(m+1)\operatorname{-an},-)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,(m+1)\operatorname{-an},-)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,m\operatorname{-an},-)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{2}\left(K^{G}_{1},\kappa_{G}^{*};\operatorname{cusp}\right)^{(\ddagger,m\operatorname{-an},-)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\operatorname{Kl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\operatorname{Kl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{m,m+1}}

and the morphism
\rho_{m,m+1}
maps
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{G,\kappa_{G}^{*}}
into
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,(m+1)\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{G,\kappa_{G}^{*}}
.

Proof.

The factorisation follows from the fact that action of
w_{1}t_{\operatorname{Kl}}w_{1}^{-1}=t_{\operatorname{Kl}}
maps
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square,\circ}_{G,m}}(\kappa_{G})
into
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square,\circ}_{G,m+1}}(\kappa_{G})
(because conjugation by
t_{\operatorname{Kl}}^{-1}
contracts the roots
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
*&1&&\\
*&&1&\\
*&*&*&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
). The integrality of the map
\rho_{m,m+1}
follows from the fact that the Hecke operator corresponding to
s_{\operatorname{Kl}}
is optimally normalised. Indeed, the cohomological correspondence
(w_{1}^{-1}\star\mathbf{1})(s_{\operatorname{Kl}})\psi_{s_{\operatorname{Kl}}}=\psi_{s_{\operatorname{Kl}}}
in Definition
4.3.7
is integral because
t_{\operatorname{Kl}}
maps
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square,\circ}_{G,m}}(\kappa_{G})^{\circ}
into
\mathbb{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square,\circ}_{G,m+1}}(\kappa_{G})^{\circ}
, and the trace map associated with the morphism
p_{1}
in the Hecke correspondence is integral by the comparison between higher Coleman theory and higher Hida theory in
[
BP
, §6]
.
∎

We now let
M_{5}\geq 0
be an integer such that
p^{M_{5}}\eta^{(1)}
is integral. From the factorisation in Lemma
7.0.6
, we see that

p^{M_{5}}\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}^{m}\eta^{(m)}=\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}s_{\operatorname{Kl}}^{m-1}\cdot(p^{M_{5}}\eta^{(m)})=\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}\rho_{m-1,m}\rho_{m-2,m-1}\cdots\rho_{1,2}(p^{M_{5}}\eta^{(1)})\;\in\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{G,\kappa_{G}^{*}}

for any
m\geq 1
, where we have used the fact that
\eta^{(m)}
is an eigenvector for the action of
s_{\operatorname{Kl}}
with eigenvalue
\alpha_{\operatorname{Kl}}
. Combining this with the discussion in the previous section completes the proof of Theorem
7.0.1
.

Appendix A
Local zeta integral computations

Let
p
be a prime. In this section, we explain how the results in
[
LPSZ21
, §8]
can be applied to compute certain local zeta integrals at Iwahori level (rather than at Klingen level). To be consistent, we will (mostly) use the same notation as in
loc.cit.
in this section; the notation will therefore differ slightly from the main body of this article.

Let
\psi\colon\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denote the standard additive character which is trivial on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(but not
p^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
). Let
\pi
be an unramified smooth irreducible representation of
\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is unitary. Let
\sigma
be a unitary unramified principal series representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and let
\chi\colon\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a finite-order character of conductor
p^{r}
(
r\geq 0
). We assume that both
\pi
and
\sigma
are generic, and we fix a Whittaker model
W_{\pi}(-)
(resp.
W_{\sigma}(-)
) with respect to
\psi
(resp.
\psi^{-1}
) of
\pi
(resp.
\sigma
). Following
[
LPSZ21
, §8.4]
, we write
\pi=I_{P_{\operatorname{Kl}}}^{G}(\tau\boxtimes\theta)
as the normalised induction from the upper-triangular Klingen parabolic subgroup to
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of a smooth irreducible unramified representation
\tau\boxtimes\theta
of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}\times\operatorname{GL}_{1}
(which we identify with the Levi of
P_{\operatorname{Kl}}
in the same way as in
loc.cit.
). We identify the generic representation
\tau
with the normalised induction
I_{B_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\alpha)
, where
\alpha\colon T_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is an unramified character. Let
\alpha_{1}=\alpha(p,1)
and
\alpha_{2}=\alpha(1,p)
. We will consider the following twisted local zeta integral:

Z(\varphi,\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\int_{Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})N_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}W_{\varphi}(h)f^{\Phi}(h_{1};\chi^{2}\chi_{\pi}\chi_{\sigma},s)W_{\lambda}(h_{2})\chi(\operatorname{det}h_{2})dh

where
\varphi\in\pi
,
\lambda\in\sigma
,
\Phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2})
and
\chi_{\pi}
and
\chi_{\sigma}
denote the central characters of
\pi
and
\sigma
.

Recall that
K_{\operatorname{B}}\subset\operatorname{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes the standard upper-triangular Iwahori subgroup, and
K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
is the depth
p^{\beta}
upper-triangular Iwahori subgroup. Fix an element
\varphi\in\pi^{K_{\operatorname{B}}}
which is an eigenvector for the actions of
U_{p,\operatorname{Si}}
and
U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}
with eigenvalues
\alpha_{1}
and
p^{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}
respectively (note that
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\theta(p)\alpha_{1},\theta(p)\alpha_{2}\}
are the Satake parameters of
\pi
).

Definition A.0.1
.

For
\beta\geq 1
, we define
\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta}\in\pi^{K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}}
to be the vector

\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(p^{7/2}\alpha_{1}^{2}\alpha_{2})^{-\beta}[K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}s_{p,\operatorname{B}}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}K_{\operatorname{B}}]\cdot\varphi,\quad t_{p,\operatorname{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{3}&&&\\
&p^{2}&&\\
&&p&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\;s_{p,\operatorname{B}}=w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}t_{p,\operatorname{B}}(w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}})^{-1}

which is an eigenvector for
U_{p,\operatorname{Si}}^{\prime}
and
U_{p,\operatorname{Kl}}^{\prime}
with eigenvalues
p^{3/2}\alpha_{1}
and
p^{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}
respectively. Moreover, one has
\operatorname{Tr}_{\beta}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\beta+1})=\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}
where
\operatorname{Tr}_{\beta}\colon\pi^{K_{\operatorname{B},\beta+1}}\to\pi^{K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}}
denotes the (unnormalised) trace map. Under the identifications above, we can view
\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}\colon G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\left(\tau\boxtimes\theta\right)\otimes\delta_{P_{\operatorname{Kl}}}^{1/2}
as the (unique up to scalar) function which is invariant under
K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}
, supported on
P_{\operatorname{Kl}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}
, and satisfies
\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}(1)=\delta_{P_{\operatorname{Kl}}}^{1/2}\cdot(\xi_{\beta}\otimes\theta)
, where
\xi_{\beta}\in\tau^{K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}
is supported on
B_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. The functions
\xi_{\beta}
are compatible under the unnormalised trace maps from level
K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta+1})
to
K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
.

We have the following result:

Proposition A.0.2
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma
be any element which is fixed by
B_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and suppose that
\Phi=\Phi_{p,\chi^{-1},\chi}
with notation as in
[
LPSZ21
, Definition 7.5]
. For
\beta\gg 2
sufficiently large (depending on
\lambda
and
\chi
):

(1)

Z(\gamma\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=0
;

(2)

One has

Z(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=p^{-4\beta}\cdot C\cdot\frac{L(\tau\times\sigma,\chi,s)}{L(\tau^{\vee}\times\sigma^{\vee},\chi^{-1},1-s)\varepsilon(\tau\times\sigma,\chi,s)}W_{\lambda}(1)

where
C\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is a non-zero scalar independent of
\chi
,
\beta
,
s
, and
\lambda
. Here
L(\tau\times\sigma,\chi,s)
(resp.
\varepsilon(\tau\times\sigma,\chi,s)
) denotes the Rankin–Selberg local
L
-factor (resp. Langlands–Deligne local factor) associated with the representation
\tau\boxtimes(\sigma\otimes\chi)
of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proof.

Note that the support of
\Phi
is contained in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\oplus\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Following exactly the same proof as in
[
LPSZ21
, Proposition 8.15]
, with the only modification being that one can substitute the conclusion of
[
LPSZ21
, Lemma 8.18]
with “
k_{a}(u,v,w)\in K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}
” (since
k_{a}(u,v,w)
lies in the upper-triangular depth
p^{\beta}
Klingen parahoric subgroup if and only if it lies in
K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}
because the
(3,2)
-th entry of
k_{a}(u,v,w)
is zero), one has the formula

Z(\gamma\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=(\star)\cdot\int_{x\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}W_{\xi_{\beta}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)W^{\Phi}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)W_{\lambda}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\chi(x)\tfrac{\theta(x)}{|x|}d^{\times}x

where
(\star)
is a non-zero scalar multiplied by the inverse of the
\gamma
-factor associated with
\tau\boxtimes(\sigma\otimes\chi)
. Note that from
[
LPSZ21
, Remark 8.2]
and the definition of
\Phi
, one can easily compute that:

W^{\Phi}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)=|x|^{s}\int_{a\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}\Phi^{\prime}(xa,a^{-1})\chi^{2}\chi_{\pi}\chi_{\sigma}(a)|a|^{2s-1}d^{\times}a=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi^{-1}(x)&\text{ if }x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\end{array}\right..

We therefore see that
Z(\gamma\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=(\star)\cdot W_{\xi_{\beta}}(1)W_{\lambda}(1)
. To conclude the proof of part (1), it suffices to show that
W_{\xi_{\beta}}(1)=0
. But this can be explicitly computed:

(A.0.3)

\displaystyle W_{\xi_{\beta}}(1)=\int_{u\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\xi_{\beta}\left(\eta\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&u\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\psi^{-1}(u)du=\sum_{h\geq\beta}\xi_{\beta}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-p^{h}&1\\
&-p^{-h}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\int_{p^{-h}\mathbb{Z}_{p}-p^{-h+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\psi^{-1}(u)du=0

using the equality
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1/m&1\\
&-m\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
1/m&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&m\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
for
m\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
(recall that
\beta\geq 2
so the integral over
p^{-h}\mathbb{Z}_{p}-p^{-h+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in (
A.0.3
) vanishes).

For the proof of part (2), let
K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the subgroup of elements which land in
P_{\operatorname{Kl}}
modulo
p^{\beta}
. Then one can easily compute that

\left(K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}\cap\gamma^{-1}H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\gamma\right)\backslash K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}/K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}=\{1,w_{1}\}

i.e., this double coset space has two representatives given by
1
and
w_{1}
. One can check that
K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}\cap\gamma^{-1}H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\gamma=K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}\cap\gamma^{-1}H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\gamma
, and

[K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}\cap\gamma^{-1}H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\gamma:w_{1}K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}w_{1}^{-1}\cap K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}\cap\gamma^{-1}H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\gamma]=p^{\beta}

with a set of representatives of
\left(\gamma K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}\gamma^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\right)/\left(\hat{\gamma}K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\right)
given by

\left\{\delta_{a}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&&\\
&1&a&\\
&&1&\\
&&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right):\text{ for }a\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right\}.

Let
T\colon\pi^{K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}}\to\pi^{K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}}
denote the unnormalised trace map. Then the above discussion implies that

\gamma\cdot T(\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime})=\gamma\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}+\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{a}\cdot\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}

hence we see that

Z(\gamma\cdot T(\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}),\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=Z(\gamma\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)+\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}}Z(\delta_{a}\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=p^{\beta}Z(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s).

Noting that
T(\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime})
is supported on
P_{\operatorname{Kl}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}
and its value at the identity is equal to
\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}
evaluated at the identity (because
B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}\cap K_{\operatorname{Kl},\beta}=K_{\operatorname{B},\beta}
), we can apply
[
LPSZ21
, Proposition 8.15]
to find that

Z(\gamma\cdot T(\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime}),\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)=\frac{p^{3-3\beta}\xi_{\beta}(1)}{(p+1)^{2}(p-1)\gamma(\tau\times\sigma_{\chi},s)}W_{\xi}(1)W_{\lambda}(1)

where
\gamma(\tau\times\sigma_{\chi},s)
is the
\gamma
-factor associated with the representation
\tau\boxtimes\sigma_{\chi}=\tau\boxtimes(\sigma\otimes\chi)
, and
\xi\in\tau
is the spherical vector normalised so that
\xi(1)=1
. We now take
C=\tfrac{p^{3}\xi_{\beta}(1)}{(p+1)^{2}(p-1)}W_{\xi}(1)
which is independent of
\beta
,
\chi
,
s
, and
\lambda
.
∎

Let
\{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\}
denote the Satake parameters of
\sigma
.

Corollary A.0.4
.

Suppose that
\lambda
is fixed by
K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p)
and that
W_{\lambda}(1)\neq 0
(e.g., one can take
\lambda
to be an eigenvector under the action of
[K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p)]
). Suppose that
\Phi=\Phi_{p,\chi^{-1},\chi}
, and set
m\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{max}(1,r)
. Then for any
\beta\geq\operatorname{max}(2m,m+1)
, one has

Z(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\varphi_{\beta}^{\prime},\lambda,\Phi;\chi,s)\doteq p^{-4\beta}\cdot\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\prod_{i,j=1}^{2}\frac{(1-p^{s-1}\alpha_{i}^{-1}\mu_{j}^{-1})}{(1-p^{-s}\alpha_{i}\mu_{j})}&\text{ if }r=0\\
G(\chi^{-1})^{-4}p^{4rs}(\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\mu_{1}\mu_{2})^{-2r}&\text{ if }r\geq 1\end{array}\right.

where
\doteq
means up to a non-zero scalar which is independent of
\beta
,
\chi
, and
s
.

Proof.

By analysing the proof of
[
LPSZ21
, Proposition 8.15]
and using the fact that
\Phi
has support in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\oplus p^{-m}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we see that the conclusion of Proposition
A.0.2
(2) holds for
\beta\geq\operatorname{max}(2m,m+1)
. The result now follows from the definition of local Rankin–Selberg
L
-factors and
\varepsilon
-factors for principal series representations.
∎

Appendix B
Nearly overconvergent modular forms

In this appendix, we study some integrality properties of nearly overconvergent modular forms which arise from analysing the various bounds in
[
GPR23
, §4]
. We begin with some notation. Let
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
denote the space of nearly overconvergent modular forms as in
op.cit.
; explicitly it can be described as
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{U}\operatorname{H}^{0}(U,\mathcal{O}_{U})
as
U
varies over all open subsets of
P^{\operatorname{an}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}
(viewed as a torsor over
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{p}K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}))
) containing the closure of
\mathcal{IG}^{\operatorname{tor}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id},\beta}
. Note that both the Igusa tower and
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
are independent of
\beta\geq 1
(see
[
GPR23
, Lemma 2.4.1]
).

For any
n\geq 1
, one has a reduction of structure
\pi_{n}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},n}\to U_{n}
of
P^{\operatorname{an}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR}}
to a
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},n}
-torsor, where
U_{n}
is a sufficiently small strict neighbourhood of
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id},\beta}
in
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}(K^{p}K^{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}))
. We set:

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{V\subset U_{n}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(V,(\pi_{n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},n}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},\circ}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{V\subset U_{n}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(V,(\pi_{n})_{*}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},n}}\right)

where both colimits are over all strict neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id}}=\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id},\beta}
contained in
U_{n}
. Both
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}
and
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},\circ}
are independent of
\beta
(and the choice of reduction of structure). Furthermore, we have
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an}}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,n\operatorname{-an},\circ}[1/p]
. Finally, we set
\mathscr{N}^{n\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{HT}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id}},(\pi_{n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},n}}\right)
and similarly for
\mathscr{N}^{n\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{HT}}
.

Proposition B.0.1
.

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1/(p-1)
and let
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the Banach space of
\varepsilon
-analytic functions
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as in
[
GPR23
, §4.1]
. Set
\upsilon(\varepsilon)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-\operatorname{log}_{p}(\varepsilon)
and let
[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]\in\mathbb{Z}
denote its integer part. Let
\star
denote the action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}
as in Theorem A of
op.cit.
. Then there exist integers
M_{1},M_{2}\geq 1
(which are independent of
\varepsilon
) such that:

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\star\left(\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}\right)\subset p^{-2[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]-M_{2}}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}

for all
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

We first claim a similar statement holds over the ordinary locus, namely there exist integers
M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime}\geq 1
(independent of
\varepsilon
) such that

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\star\left(\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{HT}}^{([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1}^{\prime})\operatorname{-an},\circ}\right)\subset p^{-2[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]-M_{2}^{\prime}}\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{HT}}^{([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1}^{\prime})\operatorname{-an},\circ}.

For this, let
\nabla
denote the operator corresponding to the identity function
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. One has an isometry

\mathscr{N}^{m\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{HT}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{N}^{m\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{HT},\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},m}^{\infty\operatorname{-ord}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},m}^{\infty\operatorname{-ord}}}\right)

where
\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},m}^{\infty\operatorname{-ord}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},m}\times_{U_{m}}\mathcal{IG}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id}}^{\operatorname{tor}}
. Let
|\!|-|\!|_{m}
denote the Banach norm on
\mathscr{N}^{m\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{HT},\infty}
with unit ball given by
\mathscr{N}^{m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{HT},\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},m}^{\infty\operatorname{-ord}},\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{dR},m}^{\infty\operatorname{-ord}}}\right)
. It suffices to prove a similar statement for these spaces.

Note that, on
\mathscr{N}^{m\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{HT},\infty}
(or more precisely on the versions defined locally with respect to opens in
\mathfrak{IG}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2},\operatorname{id}}
), the operator
\nabla
(which is integral) is congruent modulo
p^{m}
to a “nilpotent operator”
\nabla^{\prime}
(as in
[
GPR23
, §4.3]
) extending the Atkin–Serre operator. We now analyse the construction of the
\varepsilon
-analytic action as in Proposition 5.2.3 of
op.cit.
. The construction of this action relies on two key results (Proposition 4.3.4 and Proposition 4.2.4 in
op.cit.
).

Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1/(p-1)
, and let
C_{\varepsilon/2}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
be such that

p^{-k\varepsilon/2}\left|\!\left|\binom{\nabla^{\prime}}{k}\right|\!\right|_{m}\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}.

for all
k\geq 0
. We know that this exists by
[
GPR23
, Proposition 4.3.4]
– in fact analysing the proof further (and using the fact that

\binom{k-r}{k_{1},\dots,k_{\ell}}\binom{k}{r}=\binom{k}{k_{1},\dots,k_{\ell},r}

so its
p
-adic valuation is bounded by
\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)
), we can take
C_{\varepsilon/2}
to be such that
p^{-k\varepsilon/2+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)}=kp^{-k\varepsilon/2}\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}
for all
k\geq 1
. It is easy to show that
C_{\varepsilon/2}=\varepsilon^{-1}(2\operatorname{ln}(p)^{-1})
satisfies this property, where
\operatorname{ln}(-)
denotes the natural logarithm.

Take
M_{1}^{\prime}\geq 1/(p-1)+\operatorname{log}_{p}(2\operatorname{ln}(p)^{-1})+1
, then we note that
N_{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1}^{\prime}
satisfies
p^{-N_{\varepsilon}+1/(p-1)}\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}^{-1}
. By analysing the proof of
[
GPR23
, Proposition 4.2.4]
(and again using the fact that

\binom{k}{a}\binom{a}{k_{1},\dots,k_{r}}=\binom{k}{k-a,k_{1},\dots,k_{r}}

so its
p
-adic valuation is bounded by
\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)
), we see that

p^{-k\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\binom{\nabla}{k}\right|\!\right|_{N_{\varepsilon}}\leq p^{-k\varepsilon/2+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)}C_{\varepsilon/2}\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}^{2}

for all
k\geq 1
. Take
M_{2}^{\prime}\geq 2+2\operatorname{log}_{p}(2\operatorname{ln}(p)^{-1})
. One can then calculate that

C_{\varepsilon/2}^{2}\leq p^{2[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{2}^{\prime}}.

Putting this all together, we see that for any
f\in C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, one has

|\!|f\star-|\!|_{[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1}^{\prime}}\leq p^{2[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{2}^{\prime}}

which proves the claim over the ordinary locus.

Let
M_{1}\geq M_{1}^{\prime}+1
and
M_{2}\geq M_{2}^{\prime}+1
. We can write
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an}}
as an injective direct limit of Banach spaces
V_{0}\to V_{1}\to\cdots\to V_{\infty}=\mathscr{N}^{([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{HT}}
satisfying the property in
[
GPR23
, Proposition 4.4.1]
. Let
\gamma<\varepsilon
be such that
\upsilon(\gamma)+M_{1}^{\prime}\leq\upsilon(\varepsilon)+M_{1}
and
[\upsilon(\gamma)]=[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]
. Then we know that we have an action of
C_{\gamma}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\mathscr{N}^{([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an}}_{\operatorname{HT}}=V_{\infty}
, hence Proposition 4.4.1 in
op.cit.
implies that
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an}}
carries an action of
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Furthermore, given
x\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}
and
f\in C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we have that
f\star x
lies in
p^{-(2[\upsilon(\gamma)]+M_{2}^{\prime})}\mathscr{N}^{([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{HT}}=p^{-(2[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{2}^{\prime})}\mathscr{N}^{([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}_{\operatorname{HT}}
, hence we must have
f\star x\in p^{-(2[\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{2})}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,([\upsilon(\varepsilon)]+M_{1})\operatorname{-an},\circ}
. This concludes the proof.
∎
